# Changelog · October 2026

Entries from October 2026, moved here from `CHANGELOG.md` unchanged on 2026-11-20. Newest first. Phase and stage names follow the old PRD (`docs/archive/spine-prd.md`) where an entry predates the story ids.

### 2026-10-24 — upgrade.sh hands over to the new version's own script (US-OPS-50)

- **Found by the v1.0.0-beta.2 tag run:** upgrade-verify stopped before publishing anything. It renamed beta.1's `default.env.example` only for files from a local folder, not for files downloaded from GitHub. Behind that was a real problem: a beta.1 install runs beta.1's `upgrade.sh`, which can't take beta.2's renamed `env.example`.
- **Fix:** once the new release's files are downloaded and checked, `upgrade.sh` hands over, once, to the new version's own `upgrade.sh`, so an older script can't trip over a newer release again. beta.1's script predates this, so from beta.1 you take the new `upgrade.sh` first (one command in `docs/beta.md`); upgrade-verify does the same.
- **Tests:** a new script test for the hand-over (25 in all). upgrade-verify passes locally from the real beta.1 release files to beta.2, keeping the trial balance and all 35 files.

### 2026-10-24 — 1.0.0-beta.2: the release's env.example keeps its name

- **What was wrong in 1.0.0-beta.1:** GitHub renames a release file whose name starts with a dot, so `.env.example` was published as `default.env.example`. `install.sh` from a downloaded release then stopped ("env.example is missing"). Workaround for beta.1: `mv default.env.example .env.example`.
- **Fix:** releases publish it as `env.example`, and the checksums and `upgrade.sh`'s downloads use that name. `install.sh` takes `.env.example` (a clone) or `env.example` (a release). upgrade-verify renames beta.1's file when it installs beta.1 as the previous release.
- **Version:** `1.0.0-beta.2`, in the root `package.json` and both Compose files.
- **Tests:** `us-ops-01` now installs from both names, and `us-ops-50` uses `env.example`.

### 2026-10-24 — h4loom.com, Mac-built beta apps, and Spine on Coolify (D-271)

- **Domain and app id:** `h4loom.com` (home page), `docs.h4loom.com`, `beta.h4loom.com` and `demo.h4loom.com`. The field app is `com.h4loom.spine` on iOS and Android, set in `app.json` and the Maestro flows. Q-15 is closed.
- **Beta apps built on the Owner's Mac, no Expo account:** `scripts/mobile/build-beta.sh` replaces the EAS job. It runs `expo prebuild`, then Xcode archives and uploads to App Store Connect for TestFlight, and Gradle builds a signed 64-bit ARM APK (about 55 MB) attached to the release with `--upload`. The build number is the commit count (`SPINE_BUILD_NUMBER` in `app.config.js`). `eas.json` is gone. The store builds move to GitHub's macOS runners once the repo is public (US-MOB-29).
- **Found by a real Android build:** Gradle and Xcode start Expo's bundler with plain `node`, so under pnpm Babel couldn't find its presets. The script puts pnpm's hoisted packages on `NODE_PATH`, as pnpm's own launcher does. The iOS half isn't run here: it needs your Apple team.
- **Spine on Coolify:** `docker/coolify/docker-compose.yml` (published as `docker-compose.coolify.yml`) is the stack for a Coolify server:
  - Spine's Caddy runs behind Coolify's proxy on port 80, with no published ports;
  - the domain is set on the `caddy` service;
  - the settings are explicit environment variables;
  - Coolify generates `AUTH_SECRET` and the database password;
  - the Caddyfile is inline.

  The Caddyfile now takes `SITE_ADDRESS` (default `APP_URL`) and `TRUSTED_PROXIES`, so visitors' own addresses reach the API through a proxy. `docs/self-hosting-coolify.md` covers deploying, backups, upgrading, restoring, and other proxied platforms. Tested locally behind a stand-in proxy: health, sign-up and the web app.
- **Backups for scheduled tasks:** `backup.js --remote-only` names the archive itself, uploads it encrypted to `BACKUP_REMOTE` and keeps no local copy.
- **Tests:** `us-ops-52` script tests keep the Coolify file in step with the main stack and the Caddyfile. `us-mob-28` checks the build number and the id. QA sections 265 (rewritten) and 266.

### 2026-10-24 — Backups keep going past a file their storage can't address (US-OPS-03)

- **What failed:** a file record whose key the local driver won't use (an old S3-style key with the file's name in it) stopped the whole backup. Such files are now listed as missing in the manifest, like files that are gone. A real read error (disk or network) still fails the backup.
- **Archive layout:** files sit in the archive at `files/<id>`, not at their key. A key comes from the database, so it never becomes a path; the manifest maps each id to the key it's restored to.
- **Tests:** `us-ops-03` adds a record with such a key. Found by `pnpm verify`.

- **One version for the app too:** `apps/mobile/app.config.js` takes it from the root `package.json`. The stores get `1.0.0`, and the full `1.0.0-beta.1` is in `extra.spineVersion`, so Profile reads "Spine 1.0.0-beta.1 (12) · Beta". The bundle id and the EAS project can be set from the environment, ready for the final id from the domain.

### 2026-10-24 — Upgrade Spine with one command (US-OPS-50)

- **`upgrade.sh`:** it backs up, then takes the new version's files, downloaded from the GitHub release or from `--files <folder>`, checked against `SHA256SUMS` and keeping the old ones in `.previous/`. It sets `SPINE_VERSION` in `.env`, pulls the images and restarts, then prints "Spine is now <version>. Your backup is <file>." It never goes back a version, and stops before changing anything if the backup fails or the files don't check out. If the new version doesn't start, it says the data is safe and how to go back.
- **A snapshot before migrations (D-264):** when an upgrade brings migrations and there's data, the API writes a `pg_dump` snapshot to the new `spine_snapshots` volume first (the last three are kept). In production it won't migrate without one. All pending migrations run in one transaction, so a failure leaves the database as it was, and the log names the snapshot.
- **Self-hosting checks in CI:** `restore-verify.yml` becomes `self-hosting.yml`. It builds the images once, runs restore-verify, then upgrade-verify (`scripts/ci/upgrade-verify.sh`), which installs the previous release with the demo business, upgrades it with `upgrade.sh` and compares the trial balance and files. It runs on every tag before "Release", and nightly once public.
- **Found while testing:**
  - `upgrade.sh` overwrote itself while running and stopped early; files are now renamed into place.
  - On macOS `$target…` read the `…` as part of the variable's name. A script test now guards against that pattern.
- **Tests:** `us-ops-50`: snapshots, keeping three, no migration without a snapshot, and a failed migration changing nothing (integration); 7 script tests of `upgrade.sh`. upgrade-verify passes locally from a stand-in previous version. QA section 263.

### 2026-10-23 — Restore a backup into a clean install (US-OPS-04)

- **`restore.sh <archive>`:** stops Spine and streams the archive into a one-off API container. It checks everything before changing anything:
  - the passphrase;
  - the version: a backup from a newer Spine is refused, with a message to upgrade first;
  - every checksum;
  - that the install is empty (`--replace` otherwise);
  - that `AUTH_SECRET` matches the backup's fingerprint (`--new-secret` otherwise).

  Then it replaces the database with `pg_restore`, writes each file back through its storage driver and reads it back to check its checksum, and starts Spine again. A backup from an older Spine is brought up to date by the start-up migrations.
- **`restore-verify` (NFR-REL-03):** `scripts/ci/restore-verify.sh`:
  1. installs from the release's files with this commit's images;
  2. seeds the demo business and waits for the issued PDFs;
  3. makes an encrypted backup, wipes the install and installs it again;
  4. restores, and compares the trial balance and the file count.

  CI runs it on every tag before "Release". `nightly.yml` runs it every night once the repo is public. Locally: 35 files and the trial balance match, in about 2 minutes.
- **Found by it:** the slim API image (US-OPS-01) had left out `apps/api/assets`, the PDF fonts and the demo photos, so loading demo data, and issuing documents, failed in the image. The image now includes them.
- **Tests:** `us-ops-04`, an integration test covering version order, passphrases, a newer backup, a different secret, a full restore matching every table and every file, and `--replace`. QA section 262.

### 2026-10-23 — Back up everything into one file (US-OPS-03)

- **`backup.sh`:** one command writes `backups/spine-backup-<time>.tar.gz` (readable only by you). The archive holds a `pg_dump` of the database, every stored file (read through its storage driver, so the local volume or the S3 bucket alike) and a manifest with the version and checksums. A missing file is listed rather than stopping the backup. Any failure exits non-zero and leaves nothing behind. `--help` has a nightly cron line.
- **Encryption and off-site copies (D-105):** `--passphrase` or `BACKUP_PASSPHRASE` encrypts with AES-256-GCM (scrypt key). `BACKUP_REMOTE` uploads encrypted archives to S3, R2 or B2 and keeps 7 daily and 4 weekly copies.
- **Runs in the API container:** the host needs only Docker. The image gains the Postgres 16 client, which restores and US-OPS-50's snapshots will use too.
- **The Owners' banner:** "Your last backup was <n> days ago. Run a backup to keep your data safe." when it's over 2 days, from the new `GET /org/backup-status`.
- **Tests:**
  - `us-ops-03`: API unit tests (encryption, tampering, pruning, remote settings), an integration test (a real archive, encryption, failure cleanup, the status route) and a web test (the banner);
  - a real backup from an installed stack.
- **QA section 261.**

### 2026-10-23 — Install Spine with one command (US-OPS-01)

- **`install.sh`:** POSIX shell, shellcheck-clean, run from a release's files or a clone. It checks for Docker 24+ and Compose 2.20+ and names what's missing or too old. It creates `.env` (mode 600) with a random `AUTH_SECRET` and database password and the address you give, or keeps an existing `.env` untouched. It pulls the images and waits until Spine is healthy, then prints the address and the commands for logs, backup, upgrade and stop. `--non-interactive` and `APP_URL` mean it asks nothing.
- **Migrations at start (D-264):** the API applies pending migrations before serving, under an advisory lock. A fresh database gets every table, and two instances starting together don't both migrate.
- **A real install works:** found and fixed through an install from release-style files with locally built images: 15 seconds to a running stack, then sign-up, sign-in in a browser, dashboard and onboarding.
  - Sign-in returned 404 behind Caddy, because Better Auth took `/api` from `API_URL` as its route prefix. Better Auth now uses the origin, and Caddy passes `/api/auth/*` through unchanged.
  - `.env.example`'s `API_URL` is `<address>/api`, and its unused `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` is gone.
  - Compose names the release images (`ghcr.io/haribrothers/spine-*:${SPINE_VERSION:-<version>}`), and `docker compose build` still builds from source.
- **Smaller images:** the web image runs Next.js's standalone server (3.3 GB to 367 MB). The API image installs production dependencies only, without the peer packages it never loads, and ships the migrations' SQL without drizzle-kit's snapshots (2.8 GB to 1.05 GB).
- **Tests:** `us-ops-01`: installer tests against a fake `docker` (`pnpm test:scripts`), and an integration test of migrations on fresh databases. QA section 260.

### 2026-10-23 — Publish a release with one tag (US-OPS-51)

- **One tag, everything published:** on a `v*` tag, once every CI check has passed (E2E included), the new `release.yml` builds `ghcr.io/haribrothers/spine-api` and `spine-web` natively per platform. It tags them with the version (and `latest` unless it's a pre-release), and creates the GitHub release with the scripts, a Compose file that pulls those images, the Caddyfile, `.env.example` and SHA-256 checksums.
- **One version:** the root `package.json` carries the product version (`1.0.0-beta.1`), and the tag must match it.
- **One image for every domain:** the web image is built with `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL=/api`, resolved against the page's origin. The web container calls the API inside the stack (`API_URL=http://api:4000`). Before this, a built web image pointed browsers at `localhost:4000`.
- **Tests:**
  - `us-ops-51`: script tests run with `pnpm test:scripts`, now also in CI's static job; a web test covers the address;
  - both images built locally;
  - actionlint is clean.
- **QA section 259.**

### 2026-10-23 — Log every request with an id (US-OPS-07)

- **Request ids:** every response carries `X-Request-Id`, exposed to the web app for the error page (US-OPS-13). A plain id sent by a proxy is kept.
- **Logs:** one logger for the API and Better Auth. It writes JSON lines in production (time, level, request id, context, message), plus one line per request with method, path, status and duration. Elsewhere it writes readable lines; `LOG_FORMAT` and `LOG_REQUESTS` override this.
- **Nothing secret:** every message and stack is redacted of passwords, tokens, secrets, cookies, IBANs and full card or account numbers. Request bodies and query strings are never logged, and token-like path segments are replaced.
- **Tests:** `us-ops-07` (unit: redaction, paths and line shapes; integration: headers, levels, and no secrets after sign-in). QA section 258.

### 2026-10-23 — Report health and readiness (US-OPS-05)

- **`GET /health/ready`:** 200 when the database and the job queue answer, each within 3 seconds; otherwise 503, naming what isn't reachable ("The database isn't reachable."). It still reports file storage (US-OPS-31).
- **Docker Compose:** the `api` service has a healthcheck on `/health/ready`, and `web` starts only once the API is healthy.
- **Tests:** `us-ops-05` (integration: ok, database down, queue down, both; unit: timeouts and the Compose healthcheck). QA section 257.

### 2026-10-23 — Phase 12 begins; the field app ships in 1.0 on Expo SDK 57 (D-261)

- **Phase 12 · Packaging & 1.0** starts on `phase-12-packaging`.
- **Field app in 1.0:** SDK 57.0.30 with Expo's scene switch (D-252). The Owner checked the camera, photo picker and document scanner on an iOS 27 iPhone. The upgrade to SDK 58 moves to v1.1 in the roadmap (R-25); until then, the iOS 27 Search tab label and field placement are a known gap.

### 2026-10-23 — E2E runs before a release, not on every PR (D-260)

- **CI:** pull requests into `main` run lint, typecheck, unit and integration. The E2E job runs on a `v*` tag (with everything else) or a manual run, with a 60-minute limit, and keeps its Playwright report when it fails or runs out of time. On a pull request it's skipped, which `main`'s branch protection counts as passing.
- **Why:** on CI's two-core runner E2E took 33 minutes in Phase 10 and over 45 in Phase 11's PR, failing on runner speed rather than the code (the same tests take 11–12 minutes locally). `pnpm verify` already runs it before every PR; the PR now says which commit that was.
- **Docs:** D-260 (supersedes the E2E parts of D-205 and D-241), `11-testing-release.md` (CI jobs, definition of done) and CLAUDE.md.

### 2026-10-20 — Start from a ready-made task (US-MCP-09)

- **MCP prompts:** "Chase overdue invoices" (reads receivables and drafts nothing — it suggests a reminder for each customer, for the person to send from Spine), "Summarise a job" (asks for the job's number or name) and "This week in the business".
- **Per the person's tools:** each prompt is offered only when the tools it needs were offered to that person, and its text names only those tools — so Field and Staff without money reports aren't offered chasing, and their briefing uses jobs and quotes only. Running a prompt is the agent calling those tools, as the person.
- **Tests:** `us-mcp-09` (API through the MCP SDK as Owner, Staff without money reports, and Field). QA section 256.

### 2026-10-20 — See every agent request in the audit log (US-MCP-08)

- **Every call recorded:** `agent_requests` (migration `0132`) gets one row per tool call — the person, the connection, the tool, the record it touched, the outcome (`ok`, `refused`, `error`, `rate_limited`) and how long it took. Never the arguments' text or the result.
- **Team › Audit log › "Agent request":** each entry shows when, who, the action as a sentence ("Searched", "Read a quote", "Read receivables", "Drafted a quote"), the record it touched (linked) and "<Agent> · Worked", "<Agent> · Refused — <Spine's reason>" or "Slowed down". Connecting, disconnecting and the Connected agents switch were already logged.
- **120 calls a minute per connection:** past it, `/mcp` answers 429 with `Retry-After: 60` and "Too many requests from this agent. Wait a minute and try again.", and the call is logged as slowed down. The per-session limit (D-124) still applies to the person.
- **Tests:** `us-mcp-08` (API, shared, E2E). QA section 255.

### 2026-10-20 — Have an agent start work as drafts (US-MCP-07)

- **Draft tools:** `create_lead` (adding the contact if new, the enquiry as its first note), `draft_quote` and `draft_vendor_bill` (catalogue lines take the item's price and tax; Spine computes totals) and `add_note` (contact, lead or job; Field only on their assigned jobs). Offered only with `spine:draft`, while the Owner allows drafts, and per the person's rights. Expenses aren't offered: they have no draft state.
- **Only creates:** tools POST through Spine's normal create routes as the person; the client has no PUT, PATCH or DELETE, and a unit test checks every write path. Retries reuse an Idempotency-Key derived from the connection, tool, request and arguments; 30 drafts an hour per connection.
- **"via <Agent>":** `audit_log.via` (migration `0131`), set when a request's `x-spine-agent` header is signed for its own session; quote and vendor bill Activity read "Created as a draft · via Claude for Priya Raman".
- **Tests:** `us-mcp-07` (API, unit introspection and keys, web, E2E). QA section 254.

### 2026-10-20 — Ask an agent who owes us and how the business is doing (US-MCP-06)

- **Report tools:** `receivables`, `payables`, `profit_and_loss`, `balance_sheet`, `job_profitability`, `bank_balances` and `dashboard_summary`, each calling the report's own route as the person, so the figures are the report's (from journal lines), with formatted amounts and the report's link. Each is offered only to people whose capabilities open that report; Field gets none.
- **Tests:** `us-mcp-06` compares each tool's answer with the report route's. QA section 253.

### 2026-10-20 — Ask an agent to find and read records (US-MCP-05)

- **Tools:** `search`, `list_records` (contacts, leads, quotes, jobs, invoices, credit notes, payments, vendor bills, vendor credits, vendor payments, expenses, catalogue items — with status, contact, job and contact filters, pages of 25 up to 100) and `get_record` (by id, or by number, bill number or reference), all read-only. Amounts come with a formatted copy in the business's locale; records with a page carry their link.
- **As the person:** tools call Spine's own routes in-process in a session kept for the connection, so sections, ownership and Field's "assigned jobs, no money" apply exactly as on the web. Only the types the person can open are offered. That session isn't a device in Password & security and ends with the connection.
- **Tests:** `us-mcp-05` (API through the MCP SDK as Owner, Staff without invoices, and Field; unit). QA section 252.

### 2026-10-20 — See and disconnect connected agents (US-MCP-04)

- **Your account › Connected agents** (every role, while the module is on): your agents with their host (verified or "Unverified name"), what each may do, when connected and last used, and Disconnect (asks first, then a toast); with none, the address to add one.
- **The Owner's Connections card** in Settings › Connected agents: everyone's agents, whose and their role; disconnecting someone's tells them (in-app and email).
- **API:** `GET/DELETE /agents/connections[/:id]` (own) and `GET/DELETE /org/agents/connections[/:id]` (Owner). Ending a connection deletes its consent and tokens and is audited; connections unused for 30 days end when a list is read or their agent calls.
- **Fix along the way:** a Settings link to a module's pane (`?settings=my-connected-agents`) no longer falls back to the first pane before the module switches have loaded.
- **Tests:** `us-mcp-04` (API, web, E2E). QA section 251.

### 2026-10-20 — Connect an AI agent to Spine (US-MCP-03)

- **Sign-in for agents:** Better Auth 1.7's MCP OAuth provider (`@better-auth/mcp`) with Client ID Metadata Documents (`@better-auth/cimd`, its safe Node transport) and dynamic registration for agents without CIMD; authorization code + PKCE S256, tokens bound to the MCP address, 15-minute JWTs. Discovery at `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/…` and `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server/…` (Caddy routes them to the API).
- **`/mcp`:** Streamable HTTP, stateless, JSON responses, `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`. Every call also checks the person's connection, that they're active, and the module (403 `MODULE_OFF` with the business's name). Tools arrive from US-MCP-05.
- **Pages:** log-in names the agent asking and continues its sign-in; the new consent page (`/oauth/consent`) says what the agent can and can't do for the person's role, marks self-registered agents "Unverified name", lets them untick drafts, and answers Allow or Cancel. `GET /agent-consent` and `/agent-consent/agent` read the signed request.
- **Connections:** `agent_connections` (migration `0130`, with Better Auth's OAuth tables) is written on Allow and audited ("Agent connection"); deactivation and password reset end them, with their consents and tokens.
- **As built (D-258):** OAuth 2.1 allows http only on localhost, so an http address that isn't this machine can't take agents — `/mcp` answers 503 and the pane's warning now says so. The challenge names both scopes; registration infers native apps for localhost callbacks.
- **Tests:** `us-mcp-03` (API with the MCP SDK client through DCR and CIMD, web, E2E through the real log-in and consent pages). QA section 250.

### 2026-10-20 — Switch connected agents on or off (US-MCP-02)

- **Settings › Connected agents** (Owners, Business group after AI assistant): one switch, off by default, that asks first in both directions; with it on, the connector address (`API_URL` + `/mcp`) with Copy, how to add it in Claude, ChatGPT and Claude Code, a warning when an `http` address can't be reached by hosted agents, and "Agents can create drafts". Settings › Modules lists it after AI assistant with "Set up in Connected agents".
- **The sixth module:** `agents` in `modules.ts` owns `/mcp`, `/agents/*`, the `agent_disconnected` notification and Your account's pane, so the module guard refuses them while it's off. `GET/PUT /org/agents` (Owner); `settings.agents` is kept out of the generic settings routes; changes are audited as "Connected agents".
- **Tests:** `us-mcp-02` (API, web, shared, E2E). QA section 249.

### 2026-10-20 — Spine on Better Auth 1.7 (US-MCP-01, D-257)

- **Upgrade, nothing visible:** `better-auth` 1.6.26 → 1.7.11 (API and web), `@thallesp/nestjs-better-auth` 2.7 → 2.8, and the CLI is now the `auth` package (`@better-auth/cli` stops at 1.4). zod moves to 4.6.7 so Spine and Better Auth share one copy — two made the exported `auth` type unnameable (TS2742).
- **Database:** `auth generate` against Spine's schema asks only for `jwks.alg` and `jwks.crv`; migration `0129` adds them, nullable. Existing signing keys keep working (null means the 1.6 default, EdDSA) and nothing rotates. No account backfill: 1.7.3 reverted the issuer-keyed accounts of 1.7.0.
- **Code:** `getIp` → `getIP` in the reset-rate-limit hook.
- **Checks:** every auth test passes unchanged; `us-mcp-01` checks the version, cookie and Bearer sessions, and that a pre-upgrade key still signs. QA section 248.

### 2026-10-19 — Phase 11 · Connected agents (MCP) specified; Packaging & 1.0 becomes Phase 12 (D-256–D-259)

- **New phase, branch `phase-11-mcp`.** People will be able to use Spine from Claude, ChatGPT, Claude Code and other MCP clients: one MCP endpoint, `<APP_URL>/api/mcp`, where an agent acts as the person who connected it. Nine stories in `docs/features/22-mcp.md` (US-MCP-01 to US-MCP-09). This narrows the "public REST API for third parties" non-goal; there's still no public REST contract or plugin API.
- **The Owner's choices (D-256):** read and drafts only — search, records, reports, and draft leads, quotes, vendor bills and notes; never issue, send, record, approve, void, match, post, delete or change settings. OAuth 2.1 through Better Auth, no API keys. Everyone can connect within their own role once an Owner switches Connected agents on (off by default). Expenses aren't a draft tool: they have no draft state (D-259).
- **Better Auth 1.7 first (D-257):** the MCP authorization spec (2026-07-28) prefers Client ID Metadata Documents, which Better Auth supports from 1.7; the upgrade is US-MCP-01, a commit of its own.
- **Wiring and limits (D-258):** stateless Streamable HTTP in the NestJS API with `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`; 15-minute JWTs for the MCP address, rotating refresh tokens, and a per-request connection check, so disconnecting is immediate; 120 calls a minute per connection, 30 drafts an hour; every call in `agent_requests` and the audit log without arguments' text or results.
- **Contracts:** routes and events in `05-api.md`, tables in `04-data-model.md`, a "connected agents" section and principle 5's outbound call in `07-security.md`, risks R-27 to R-29, the tech stack, the overview's non-goal and the roadmap.
- **Renumbering:** Packaging & 1.0 is Phase 12 in the phase plan, progress, feature files, QA script, risks and code comments; past decisions and changelog entries keep their numbers (as D-195 did). The Expo SDK 58 upgrade (R-25) follows Phase 11.
- **Screens:** ten, drawn in Claude Design (project "Spine web — Phase 11 connected agents", canvases 01 Settings, 02 Connect, 03 Connections and audit) on the refined web app, with light and dark versions of the settings pane and the consent page, exported to `docs/design/screens/` and listed in `screens.md` under "Connected agents (MCP)". A draft quote an agent creates has no number until it's sent, so US-MCP-07's answer names the customer and total instead.

### 2026-10-19 — AI suggestions while reconciling (US-AI-14)

- **"Suggest with AI (<n> lines)"** beside "Match all suggested" looks at the lines the rules couldn't settle (D-158), only when pressed. AI's suggestions join the Suggested tab: "AI suggests: <entry> · <contact>" with the reason, or "AI suggests creating: <category> · <contact>". The tab says "AI suggestions aren't in Match all — check and match each one." — Match all still takes only the rules'.
- **Selecting a line** puts AI's entry first in "Unreconciled in Spine", marked "AI suggests", before "Same amount" and the nearest dates. **Create** opens with AI's kind (Other money in/out, a customer payment or a vendor payment), category and contact, each "Suggested by AI".
- **Nothing is matched or created by AI:** Match, or saving the create form, does that. A match made from AI's suggestion is saved as suggested by AI (`bank_statement_line_matches.suggested_by`, migration 0128) and reads "Matched · suggested by AI". AI's suggestions are kept on the reconciliation, so coming back doesn't ask (or spend) again; "Suggest again" replaces them.
- **What's sent:** dates, signed amounts and redacted descriptions, each line's 8 likeliest entries, recent categorised money in/out, and the categories and contacts AI may pick. A match only stands when its first entry has the line's amount — AI only ranks.
- **Tests:** API (`us-ai-14` unit and integration), web (`us-ai-14`), e2e `us-ai-14.spec.ts`. Tried live on `qwen3.5:4b`: 13–24 seconds for 4 lines, three correct matches (R-26). QA section 247.

### 2026-10-19 — Draft quote lines from an enquiry (US-AI-13)

- **"Draft lines with AI"** beside "Add item" on a new or draft quote opens a side panel: paste the enquiry, press "Draft lines", and AI proposes lines matched to the catalogue — items with their price, bundles with their total, and work that isn't in the catalogue with "Add a price". Tick the ones you want and "Add to quote".
- **Lines arrive as if picked by hand:** the item's price, its tax for the place of supply and its HSN/SAC, a bundle as its lines; each row reads "Suggested by AI" until changed, and a line without a price says so until it has one. AI never sets a price (D-172) — an item priced in another currency comes in without one. The saved quote says "filled in with AI".
- **The catalogue sent:** the 50 active items and bundles (in the quote's currency) that best match the enquiry by trigram similarity, as refs, names, units and contents — never prices; the enquiry is redacted.
- **API:** `POST /ai/draft-quote-lines` (no Idempotency-Key). Tried live on `qwen3.5:4b`: lines in 20–23 seconds — close to the 30-second limit on a bigger catalogue (R-26).
- **Tests:** API (`us-ai-13` unit and integration), web (`us-ai-13`), e2e `us-ai-13.spec.ts`. QA section 246.

### 2026-10-19 — A reminder or statement in a chosen tone (US-AI-12)

- **Tone:** reminders and statements — from an invoice, Receivables or a contact — offer Friendly, Formal and Firm beside "Draft with AI" (Friendly until changed) and in the suggestion's header, where choosing another tone drafts again in it. Other emails have no tone; the API refuses one with 400.
- **The details stay Spine's in every tone** (D-254): the balance, number, dates and days overdue are filled in by Spine after drafting. Firm is told never to threaten or add fees, interest, legal action or deadlines the template doesn't say.
- **Tests:** API (US-AI-12 prompt and filling; a reminder and a statement in a tone; 400 for an invoice email), web (`us-ai-12`), e2e `us-ai-12.spec.ts` from Receivables (the e2e AI stub now keeps the last prompt). Tried live on `qwen3.5:4b` on Ollama: every tone passed in 7–14 seconds. QA section 245.

### 2026-10-19 — Draft a customer email with AI (US-AI-11, D-254)

- **"Draft with AI" in every send dialog** — quotes, invoices, reminders, receipts, credit notes, Receivables and the statement dialog — for people who may send the email and use AI. AI's draft shows as a suggestion above the email ("Use this draft", "Try again", "Discard"); Subject and Message change only when it's used, tagged "Drafted by AI" until edited, with "Undo".
- **The document's own details, never the model's:** the model writes placeholders and Spine fills them, the same values as the template; a detail it wrote out goes back to Spine's value, and a draft with a made-up placeholder or number is refused as "AI didn't answer…". The text sent to the model is redacted (D-165); payment instructions aren't sent.
- **Highlights:** the details Spine filled in are highlighted in the Message — the template draft too — on screen only. `RichTextEditor` and `RichTextContent` take `highlights` (documented in the design system). The statement dialog now catches a placeholder Spine doesn't know, like the other send dialogs.
- **Design:** the dialog was redrawn from the app's real rich-text dialog ("05 Emails v2"): `SendEmailDialogAi`, `SendEmailDialogAiSuggestion`, `SendEmailDialogAiUsed`. D-171 amended.
- **API:** `POST /ai/draft-email` (no Idempotency-Key — it saves nothing); `/email-drafts` and the statement draft return `values`. Drafting runs at temperature 0.7 so "Try again" differs.
- **Tests:** API (`us-ai-11` unit and integration; US-OPS-08, US-SET-21 and statements suites), web (`us-ai-11`), `@spine/ui` highlights a11y, e2e `us-ai-11.spec.ts`. QA section 244.
- **After trying it on a local model (D-255):** a "thinking" model (`qwen3.5:4b` on Ollama) spent 90 seconds reasoning and every draft timed out (504). The OpenAI-compatible driver now asks models not to think (`reasoning_effort: "none"`, dropped for a server that refuses it), so drafts — and bill and receipt reads — come back in seconds. The number check compares whole numbers, so "is currently 35 days overdue" passes while a made-up figure still doesn't. Tested live: 5 of 5 drafts in 6–8 seconds.

### 2026-10-19 — The field app opens on iOS 27, back works on Android 13–15, and Maestro flows for US-AI-10 (D-252, D-253)

- **iOS 27:** the app stopped at launch on the iOS 27 simulator (iOS now requires the scene lifecycle). It moves to Expo SDK 57.0.30 and turns on Expo's own scene support (`expo-build-properties` `ios.enableSceneSupport`); the `expo-modules-core` override and the `expo-modules-jsi` patch go, as 57.1.2 fixes that build error upstream. A stopgap until SDK 58, which is tracked as R-25 before Phase 11. `@expo/ui` 57.0.25 changed how a sheet's and a border's colours are passed; the text field and two tests follow.
- **Android back (D-253):** on Android 13–15 the back gesture or button closed the app from any screen — the app opted in to predictive back, which React Native 0.86 handles only on Android 16+. It no longer opts in; Android 16+ still gets predictive back.
- **Maestro:** `us-ai-10-receipt` (iOS and Android) chooses a receipt from the library, reads it and submits; `us-ai-10-offline` (Android) checks "Needs a connection" and the queued send. `us-mob-23-search` finds the Search field where iOS 27 puts it (under the title, not in the tab bar). Android flows run on an Android 15 emulator with 4 GB: on Android 16/17 images Maestro types one key every ~5 s (Maestro #2718). How to run them is in `docs/design/native-notes.md`.
- **Tests:** iOS 27 — `us-ai-10-receipt`, `us-mob-01`, `03`, `04`, `13`, `23` pass. Android 15 — `us-ai-10-receipt`, `us-ai-10-offline`, `us-mob-07`, `11`, `13` pass, and back from a job, Sync and New expense returns a screen. US-MOB-20's test now checks the app doesn't opt in to predictive back. QA section 243 lists the flows and an Android 13–15 back check.

### 2026-10-18 — Every file link checks who uploaded it (D-250, widened; security)

- **Why:** a review of the receipt fix found the same hole on other routes: any member could link a file they may not read to a record they can — through their profile photo, a job or milestone photo, a contact's logo, or an attachment (which checked the uploader but not whether a customer's signature or a job photo already used the file).
- **Now:** one check on every link a non-Owner can make — attachments, expense receipts, job and milestone-completion photos, contact logos and people's photos, the on-site quote signature, and profile photos (both `PUT /users/me` and Better Auth's `/update-user`): the person's own upload, used by nothing else. A record keeping its own file always passes, and so does a photo already on the same job (a retried send). AI won't read a "fresh upload" that has become a job photo or a signature.
- **Along with it:** demo data uploads its own copy of a sample site photo for each job and milestone (it used to share six files between them); the AI routes that save nothing are on US-OPS-08's list of routes without an Idempotency-Key, which that check had been missing since US-AI-02.
- **Tests:** `d-250` covers each route; the whole API integration suite was run.

### 2026-10-18 — Fill in my expense from a receipt on the phone (US-AI-10, D-251)

- **"Read receipt" on New expense (iOS and Android):** under the receipt photo, in Amount mode. It uploads the photo and has AI read it; Amount, Date, Category and Description fill in, each "Suggested by AI" until changed, with "Filled in from your receipt. Check the amount, date and category before you submit." at the top. Job, Paid by and tax are left alone. "Read again" and "Retake" under the photo.
- **Offline:** "Read receipt" shows dimmed with "Needs a connection — fill it in, or read it later.", and the expense queues exactly as before. Hidden in Labour mode, with AI or the feature off, without the right to use AI, or with a model that can't read images; dimmed with the date while the budget is used up.
- **Your entries stay:** AI fills only what you haven't entered and offers "Replace my entries". It won't fill an amount in another currency (it says so), a future date, or a category that isn't yours. "Cancel", a retake or leaving drops the read; "AI didn't answer…" offers Try again.
- **One upload:** the photo AI read is the expense's receipt — the queued expense sends its id instead of uploading it again — and the expense says "filled in with AI". Nothing in the outbox ever calls AI, so hand-filled expenses sync as before.
- **Under the hood:** requests can be cancelled; AI's own 503/504 keep their message on the phone; the AI status is kept for offline use. The "Suggested by AI" tag is an app-level component, no change to the shared native components.
- **After review:** switching to Labour stops a read in progress; a category deleted since the expense was queued counts as empty; offline says so once and clears when the connection is back; VoiceOver and TalkBack hear the result; iOS "Retake" is a visible grey; the date's tag sits under its caption. The outbox now keys each upload and attach by photo, not position — a retake after a refusal, or a removed photo, used to bring back the earlier photo's file.
- **Tests:** phone (`us-ai-10`: 30 tests on iOS and Android, including the outbox and request layer; `us-mob-09` keys updated) and API (`us-ai-10`, a Field user with a bearer token). QA section 243 covers the device checks.

### 2026-10-18 — An expense's receipt must be your own upload (D-250, security)

- **The gap:** an expense stored any file id it was given as its receipt. File reads follow the record a file belongs to, so someone who knew a file's id could attach it to their own expense and read it — a Field user the customer signature they captured on site, a Staff member a file attached to a record they can't open.
- **The fix:** `POST` and `PUT /expenses` take a receipt only if the person uploaded it and nothing else uses it — the rule attachments already follow. Otherwise "Upload the receipt first." or "This file is already used elsewhere. Upload the receipt again to use it here." Keeping an expense's own receipt on edit still works. The web form, the phone and demo data already send the person's own fresh upload.
- **Tests:** API integration (`d-250`); every purchases, files, mobile, AI, attachments, banking and demo-data suite passes. Found while building US-AI-10.

### 2026-10-18 — Fill in an expense from a receipt (US-AI-09, D-249)

- **"Read receipt" on the expense form:** under the receipt preview (not in labour mode). Date, Amount, Currency, Tax rate and Tax amount, Category and Description fill in, marked "Suggested by AI", with "Filled in from the receipt. Check the fields marked Suggested by AI before you submit." above the form.
- **Category and tax, matched by Spine:** the category this merchant's last expenses used ("As on your last 3 expenses from HP Fuel Centre."), else the one whose name and "What it's for" fit. The receipt's tax as printed, with the rate of that percentage ("From the receipt: CGST ₹369.92 + SGST ₹369.91."); with no tax shown, the category's usual rate works it out as included.
- **As before:** the same-amount warning shows once the read amount and date fill; the expense submits and is approved as normal and says "filled in with AI"; "Submit and add another" starts clean; fixing a rejected expense fills only empty fields and offers "Replace my entries".
- **Field** can read the receipt on their own expense without the Team right (D-245). The unreadable-file and damaged-PDF messages now fit bills and expenses alike.
- **Tests:** API integration (`us-ai-09`), web (`us-ai-09`) and E2E (`us-ai-09`). QA section 241.

### 2026-10-17 — Spine runs on Node 22 (D-248)

- **Why:** reading a vendor's PDF (US-AI-08) failed in the Docker image — its Node 20 lacks what the PDF library needs — though it passed locally and in CI, which run newer Node. Node 20 is past end of life.
- **What changed:** the API and web images are built on `node:22-alpine`; `package.json` asks for Node 22 or later, and the README says so. Both images build, and a text PDF and a scanned PDF read inside the API image.
- **For self-hosters:** nothing to do with Docker Compose — the new images carry Node 22. Running from source needs Node 22.

### 2026-10-17 — Fill in a vendor bill from the vendor's PDF or photo (US-AI-08, D-247)

- **"Read from document" on the bill page:** choose or drop the vendor's PDF or photo at the top of the header card. It's attached to the bill, and Vendor, Bill number, Currency, Bill date, Due date, Place of supply and the lines fill in, each marked "Suggested by AI" until changed. "Read" is offered on a PDF or photo already in Attachments too.
- **Spine does the matching, not the model:** the vendor and catalogue items by name; each line's account from the vendor's last bill or the item; tax rates by percentage for the supply type; the place of supply from the vendor; a due date from the document or its payment terms, else the vendor's usual terms. Totals are Spine's own, with a warning when the document's total differs. A vendor it can't match is offered — added only when you confirm.
- **What's sent:** a PDF's text; a scanned PDF's first pages or a photo as images (rendered with `@napi-rs/canvas`, added to the API). A model that can't read images says so before anything is sent.
- **Your entries stay:** AI fills only what you haven't entered and offers the rest with "Replace my entries (<n>)". The bill saves through the normal buttons and says "filled in with AI" in its Activity.
- **Tests:** API unit (matching, document preparation) and integration (`us-ai-08`, against a stand-in provider), web (`us-ai-08`), and E2E (`us-ai-07-08`: reading a PDF into the bill, and the "AI didn't answer" state with Try again and saving by hand). QA section 240.

### 2026-10-17 — Keep working when AI is unavailable (US-AI-07, D-246)

- **AI never blocks work:** a provider that fails or can't be reached answers "AI didn't answer. Fill this in by hand or try again." at once (503), one that doesn't answer in 30 seconds the same (504); neither is retried, and forms save by hand as normal.
- **Replies are checked field by field:** each AI reply is fitted to the feature's fixed shape before it reaches a form — a field that fails its check stays empty while the rest fill, fields the shape doesn't name are dropped, and a reply that isn't JSON fills nothing (R-23).
- **The pieces every AI control uses:** the "Read from document"-style button with "Reading…" and "Cancel" (no spinner with reduced motion), the inline "AI didn't answer" message with "Try again", and the rules that a cancelled request fills nothing and AI fills only fields still empty when it answers. The API client can now cancel a request, and a feature switched off shows its own message rather than "You don't have permission".
- **Tests:** API unit (`us-ai-07.fit-reply`) and integration (`us-ai-07`, with the test provider hanging, failing and gone), and web (`us-ai-07`). The bill page's end-to-end check comes with US-AI-08. QA section 239.

### 2026-10-17 — Who may use AI, and the record of its use (US-AI-05, US-AI-06, D-245)

- **"Use AI features" for a Team:** a new `ai.use` capability in its own "AI assistant" group in each Team's permissions, off for General Staff and every existing Team (migration 0127), changed through the draft and Save confirm like any other, hidden while AI is off and kept for when it's back. The panel says AI isn't a section. Owners always may; Field may only read the receipt on their own expense — a fixed rule, not a capability. `GET /ai/status` now says `canUse` and `canReadOwnReceipt`, and the AI gateway refuses anyone else ("You don't have the right to use AI. An owner can give it to your Team.").
- **Every AI request in the audit log:** record type "AI request", with the feature as its action ("Read a document", "Drafted an email", …), the record it was for (opens it) and one line of details — provider, model, tokens and whether it worked. Never the text.
- **"Filled in with AI":** bills, expenses and quotes take `aiAssisted` on create and update; it sets the new `ai_assisted` column and marks that save, so Activity reads "Created as a draft · filled in with AI" (and "Submitted for approval", "Edited") and History "… and filled it in with AI".
- **Tests:** integration (`us-ai-05-06`), web and E2E; the shared and capability tests now expect `ai.use` to read off while AI is off. QA section 238.
- **Local note:** on this Mac `localhost` now resolves to IPv6 first while MailDev listens on IPv4 only, so local tests that send email (US-AUTH-09/10, US-TEAM-21/24, US-SET-15, signup) wait 10 s and time out; they fail the same way without these changes, and CI isn't affected.

### 2026-10-17 — AI features and the monthly budget (US-AI-03, US-AI-04, D-244)

- **A switch per feature:** with AI on, Settings › AI assistant shows Features beside Provider — "Read bills and receipts", "Draft customer emails", "Draft quote lines" and "Suggest bank matches", each with where it shows, a switch (on by default, saved as it changes, audited) and "What's sent", which lists exactly what that feature sends and what's never sent. A model that can't read images is noted on the first.
- **Monthly budget:** tokens a month (empty for no limit), with this month's usage — tokens against the budget with the percentage and a bar, requests, the top three features and people — and "AI is paused until 1 <month> — this month's budget is used up." once it's reached. Owners hear once at 80% and once at 100%.
- **One gateway:** `AiRequestService.run` is the only way a feature reaches the provider: it refuses a switched-off feature (403 `AI_FEATURE_OFF`), more than 30 requests in 10 minutes from one person (429) and a used-up budget (409), and turns a failure or a 30-second wait into "AI didn't answer. Fill this in by hand or try again." Every request, refusals included, is a row in the new `ai_requests` table (migration 0126) — never its text. `GET /ai/status` now says which features are on and whether AI is paused; `GET /org/ai/usage` gives the month's usage.
- **Redaction (D-165):** `redact()` replaces GSTINs, PANs, IFSC codes, IBANs, card numbers, tax IDs, UPI IDs, keys, tokens, passwords and long account numbers with placeholders before any text is sent; amounts, dates, document numbers and email addresses stay (20 unit tests).
- **QA:** section 237; section 236 now uses qwen3-vl:8b and qwen3:4b — `llama3.2-vision` doesn't load on current Ollama.

### 2026-10-17 — Settings › AI assistant: the switch and the provider (US-AI-01, US-AI-02, D-243)

- **One switch for all AI:** Settings › AI assistant (Business group, after Modules; Owner-only) starts off. It won't switch on without a provider ("Connect a provider first."); with a provider outside the local network it asks "Turn on AI?", names the host and lists what each feature sends and what's never sent; the acknowledgement is audited and remembered for that host. Switching off asks too. Toasts say what happened.
- **The fifth module:** AI owns `/ai/*`, which answers 403 `MODULE_OFF` while it's off; Settings › Modules lists it with its state and "Set up in AI assistant". `GET /org/modules` adds `ai: true` only while it's on, so the four Modules switches and their Save are unchanged.
- **Your own provider:** "Local or OpenAI-compatible" (server address, model, optional key — Ollama, vLLM, LM Studio or a cloud service) or "Anthropic Claude" (key and model). The Model picker lists what the provider reports and takes a typed name; Test connection says "Connected · <model> · reads images: yes/no" or "Couldn't reach <address>: <reason>. Check the address and key, then try again." Drivers call each API with `fetch` through one client with a 30-second timeout — no provider SDK.
- **The key:** sealed with AES-256-GCM under a key derived from `AUTH_SECRET`, in its own `ai_provider_key` setting; shown only as "••••" and its last four characters with Replace key; never returned, logged, audited in full or exported. The generic `/org/settings` routes no longer read or write the `ai` key or any secret key.
- **Tests:** `us-ai-01`/`us-ai-02` integration tests against a stand-in local provider (and a spy for Anthropic) prove nothing reaches a provider while AI is off; web tests for the pane; an E2E run through the whole flow with a stand-in server. US-SET-31's Business group now ends with AI assistant. QA script section 236.
- **Steadier test:** US-INV-20 looks for the "Recurring invoices" heading exactly — with no schedules yet, "No recurring invoices yet" matched too.

### 2026-10-16 — Phase 10 stories fitted to the refined web app (D-242)

- **Branch `phase-10-ai-assistant`.** Before building the AI assistant, its 14 stories (`docs/features/21-ai.md`) were rewritten against the screens the refinements rebuilt — same scope and ids, US-AI-11 renamed "Draft a customer email with AI".
- **Where AI now sits:** Settings › AI assistant in the Business group (Modules shows it as the fifth module, linking to the pane); `ai.use` in the Team permissions draft, not a section; the bill page's header card and compact rows, with the duplicate-bill check on what AI read; the expense form's first file, filling Description with the merchant and using the receipt's tax before the category's default; the shared send dialog, so every customer email can be drafted; compact quote rows; the reconcile Suggested tab, where "Suggest with AI" runs on request and "Match all suggested" stays rules only; and "filled in with AI by <name>" in each record's activity.
- **Shared rules** (marked until changed, empty fields only, hidden when not allowed, the form's own confirms and toasts) are stated once in the feature file.
- **Contracts:** `POST /org/ai/test` returns the provider's model list; AI routes name the section and record rights they also need; bank suggestions run only on request; bill, expense and quote activity entries gain `aiAssisted`. 07-security's AI section now says Phase 10.
- **Screens:** the 17 AI screens are drawn in the Claude Design project "Spine web — Phase 10 AI assistant" (canvases 01 Settings to 08 Phone) on the refined layouts, with light and dark versions of the main ones, and exported to `docs/design/screens/` (`ExpenseDialogAi` → `ExpenseFormAi`, `EmailDraftAi` → `SendEmailDialogAi`). Every AI-filled field is tinted and tagged "Suggested by AI" (icon and words); the Settings pane uses the Bot icon, since Industry template already uses Sparkles. No code changed.

### 2026-10-13 — Cheaper, steadier E2E setup (D-241)

- **Sign-in through the API:** `signUpNewOrg` and `logIn` sign up and sign in through the API instead of the sign-up, onboarding and login pages (which keep their own specs), then open the dashboard.
- **MailDev:** `clearInbox` no longer empties the inbox; it remembers what's there and `waitForEmail` ignores those.
- **Queue polling:** the API accepts `QUEUE_POLLING_SECONDS` (pg-boss's check interval). Set to 0.5 s for E2E it took the slowest email test from 23 s to 2 s locally, but in CI it ran the demo's queued emails alongside the tests on two cores and the suite passed its 30-minute limit — so it's left unset everywhere.
- **CI:** the E2E job keeps the web app's Next.js build cache between runs, and its limit goes from 30 to 45 minutes: CI's two-core runner takes about 28 minutes for the 421 tests plus 5 for setup (11.6 minutes locally), and one run stopped at test 379 with none failing.
- **Steadier tests:** US-QUO-32 waits for the quote's email before the customer accepts; US-JOB-32 waits for the contact filter to load; US-MTG-14 answers its permissions request from a copy taken first; US-AUTH-14 leaves the dashboard before clearing cookies. US-RPT-23 lets each favourite save finish before the next (each saves the whole list). US-INV-23 reminds one of the demo's own invoices (an earlier spec's invoice had a customer with no email) and checks the send's answer, not only its toast; US-RPT-15 counts the table's first page (10 jobs), since earlier specs add jobs. US-PUR-23 waits for the trades used before to load before typing; US-OPS-36 hovers again until the page is interactive; the US-PUR-54 integration test counts only the remittance email (the app's queue may send another meanwhile). Found by CI: the dashboard's short amounts (`formatMoneyCompact`) now set a minimum of no decimals, so every Node version shows "$950" rather than "$950.0"; the search palette's unit tests wait up to 5 s for results. The credit-notes integration test stubs email as the remittance test does (CI has no SMTP), and US-INV-17 checks only its own invoice's PDFs, not every file of the organisation. US-RPT-23 looks for report groups within the page's main region (a slow runner briefly had the outgoing page too); the sign-in helper no longer waits for the permissions response (in CI that wait timed out on every sign-in), and US-TEAM-18 waits for its own sidebar links instead.
- **The four flaky tests (R-24) fixed:** dashboard "Edit layout" lets each layout save land; US-INV-18 switches automatic reminders back off (their 15-minute job was reminding US-INV-23's invoice first); US-OPS-26 searches the jobs list for the demo job; and US-PUR-23's missing suggestion was a real bug — `GET /expenses/labour-trades` returned the first 20 trades alphabetically, so a business past 20 trades never saw later ones suggested. It now returns the 20 used most recently, newest first (integration test added).
- **Tried and not kept:** parallel workers (2 were ~30% faster on a sample, 4 slower, with new failures) — reasons in D-241.

### 2026-10-13 — One native doc app (D-240)

- **Removed `apps/design-system-native`**, the unused first doc app for `@spine/ui-native`, and its `dev:ui-native` script. `apps/showcase-native` is now the one native doc app (supersedes D-034).
- **Moved its two pages with no showcase demo:** OutlinedTextField and SpineLogo now have showcase demos and catalog entries; `check-doc-coverage` accepts only showcase demos, and US-MOB-27's test checks the showcase's Spine Logo demo.
- **Docs:** CLAUDE.md, native notes, the monorepo layout, tech stack, design-system rules, the native component docs (doc page links), the mobile stories and the QA script name `apps/showcase-native`. The React Navigation components only that app demonstrated (TabBar, FloatingTabBar, NativeHeader, NativeTabView) stay in `packages/ui-native`, unused and without a doc page.

### 2026-10-13 — Integration tests in parallel (D-239)

- **Faster suite:** integration files run in parallel, each Vitest worker against its own copy of the migrated `spine_test` (`spine_test_w1`…, made from it as a template before the run and dropped after). `SPINE_TEST_WORKERS` sets the count (4 locally, 1 in CI — two workers on CI's two-core runner were slower than one, 18 minutes against 13½). Locally 10½ → 4¾ minutes; no CI workflow change.
- **Connections:** `createDb` and pg-boss read `DATABASE_POOL_MAX` and `DATABASE_IDLE_TIMEOUT` (unset in production); the test workers set 5 and 2 s so every worker fits under Postgres's 100 connections.
- **Steadier tests:** the quote-view throttle test waits for its unawaited notifications instead of a fixed 50 ms, and US-OPS-28's weekly-run check reads the run's newest warning without depending on how many damaged files the database already holds.

### 2026-10-13 — Search that finds more and takes you further (US-OPS-39 to US-OPS-49, D-238)

- **Before you type (US-OPS-39):** search shows the 5 records you opened last (from search, a list, a link or the dashboard) and your last 3 searches, kept on your account (`user.recent_items`, `user.recent_searches`, migration `0125`) and read again with today's access each time; "Clear recent" empties both. `GET/POST/DELETE /search/recent`; the app layout's `RecentVisits` records each record page opened.
- **More to find (US-OPS-43 to US-OPS-46):** new groups for leads, meetings (the calendar's own visibility), catalogue items and vendor credits, in the sidebar's order; contacts by phone digits, tax ID or GSTIN and their people, with the second line saying what matched; documents by amount in the business's number format (exact total, or balance due for invoices and bills; never for Field), first in their groups and in bold; archived contacts and catalogue items with "Include archived" (remembered on the device). Trigram indexes on lead titles, meeting titles, catalogue item names, vendor credit numbers and contact people.
- **Go anywhere (US-OPS-40 to US-OPS-42):** every group ends with "See all … matching", which opens its list with the search filled in; pages (sidebar and reports) and Settings panes this person can open; "new" actions named as on each page's button. Pipeline, Meetings, Payments and Vendor payments open their create form from `?new=1`; Catalogue opens an item from `?item=`.
- **At a glance (US-OPS-47 to US-OPS-49):** each row has its section's sidebar icon, the typed text in bold and a coloured status; the footer says how to move, open and close; the empty state says what this person can search. Choosing a row keeps search open with a spinner on it until the page has loaded (`CommandPalette` `pendingId`, a navigation transition). Search is a "Search… ⌘K" field under the logo (an icon when collapsed). Chips (All, Sales, Jobs, Billing, Purchases — the segmented control) narrow to one area with up to 20 a group (`GET /search?types=`).
- **Components:** `@spine/ui` `CommandPalette` gains item icons, `highlight`, `metaTone`, `link` rows and a `toolbar`; `AppShellSidebar` gains a `search` slot. Doc pages and a11y tests updated.
- **Tests:** shared `us-ops-39-49`; API integration `us-ops-39-49`; web unit `us-ops-39-49`; UI a11y `command-palette`, `app-shell`; E2E `us-ops-39-49`, `us-ops-22` updated for the new footer and sidebar field.

### 2026-10-13 — Documents shows a sample page, not the PDF (US-SET-17 to US-SET-19, D-237)

- **Preview:** Settings › Documents draws an HTML sample of the document beside the options — the business's name or logo, the document colour and footer, the title and number, bill to, sample lines with the chosen columns (HSN/SAC and CGST/SGST for GST), totals, tax summary, amount in words, terms, notes and the uploaded signature above "Authorised signatory" — on a page of the chosen paper size, scaled into a 380 px column. It follows every option as it changes, needs nothing to load, looks the same in every browser and stays light in dark mode. It replaces the browser's PDF viewer in an iframe, which was blank in some browsers and didn't match the design.
- **Tests:** web unit `us-set-17-sample`; E2E `us-set-17` now checks the sample (title, columns, paper shape, amount in words) instead of the preview requests, and `us-set-19` checks the uploaded signature shows in it.

### 2026-10-13 — One Settings for everyone (US-AUTH-16 to US-AUTH-19, US-SET-30 to US-SET-34, D-236)

- **Your account (US-SET-30):** Profile & Preferences is now the "Your account" group of Settings — Profile, Password & security, Notifications — which every role opens from the user menu; Staff and Field see only it, and the business groups and their writes stay Owner-only. `/profile` (and `?tab=`) opens the matching pane. The Appearance tab is gone.
- **Theme (US-AUTH-16):** the user menu's Light / Dark / System saves to the account and the saved choice is applied when a tab starts, so it follows the person to every device (it never did before: the menu only changed the browser). A failed save says so and keeps the theme on this device.
- **Where you're signed in (US-AUTH-17):** Password & security lists each session (browser or the phone app, IP, last active, "This device") with Sign out and Sign out of other devices, both asking first. Changing the password signs out other devices unless unticked — this device stays signed in. `GET /users/me/sessions`, `DELETE /users/me/sessions/:id`, `POST /users/me/sessions/sign-out-others`.
- **Change email (US-AUTH-18):** "Change email" asks for the current password, refuses an address another account uses, and emails the old address (`email_changed`, migration `0124`). `PUT /users/me/email`; `PUT /users/me` no longer takes `email` — changing it there had been failing ("Change email is disabled").
- **The details (US-AUTH-19):** the photo shows in the sidebar and menu, with Remove photo; Save waits for a change and toasts; meeting reminders name the business default and the digest names the time zone (`GET /users/me` `orgDefaults`); the menu uses the standard icons.
- **Settings (US-SET-31 to US-SET-34):** grouped (Your account, Business, Money & tax, Documents & emails, Sales & meetings, Data, Team links); `@spine/ui` Dialog `size="xl"` (up to 1152 × 960, full screen on phones with the list first); the pane in the address (`?settings=<pane>`, `SettingsRoute`); a dot and "Discard unsaved changes to <pane>?" before edits are dropped; search by what panes contain, with what matched, Enter and ↑/↓.
- **Tests:** API integration `us-auth-16-19`; shared `us-auth-17`; web unit `us-set-30-34`, `us-auth-16-19`; `@spine/ui` dialog a11y (xl); E2E `us-set-30-34`, `us-auth-16-19` (with axe), with `us-ntf-12`, `us-ntf-14`, `us-ops-26`, `us-onb-15`, `us-set-09`, `us-auth-15` and `route-smoke` moved to Settings, and "Saved" matched exactly in the settings specs. QA script section 234.

### 2026-10-13 — Tabs that name their panel on Banking, Payables, Receivables and Reconcile

- **Fix:** each tab points screen readers (`aria-controls`) at the content it shows, but on Banking (Active/Archived), Payables (By vendor/By bill), Receivables (By customer/By invoice) and Reconcile's statement lines (To do/Suggested/Matched/Excluded) that content sat outside the tabs, so the tab pointed at nothing and axe failed. The content is now the tab's panel (`TabsContent`), as on Users. Nothing looks different.
- **Tests:** E2E `us-bank-13-17`, `us-pay-27-31` and `us-pur-56-60` now run axe on these pages (and after switching tabs on Receivables).

### 2026-10-13 — Sections a Team can open (US-TEAM-30 to US-TEAM-32, D-235)

- **Open permissions (US-TEAM-30):** 13 capabilities — Open Contacts, Pipeline, Meetings, Catalogue, Quotes, Jobs, Invoices, Credit Notes, Payments, Vendor Bills, Vendor Credits, Vendor Payments, and "See everyone's expenses" — first in their groups, on by default, and required by each section's actions (`CAPABILITY_REQUIRES`; turning Open off turns them off). `@RequireSectionOpen` on each section's controllers is checked as well as any `@RequireCapability`, so a closed section refuses every route; the sidebar and page access hide and refuse it (`SECTION_OPEN_CAPABILITIES`). Without "See everyone's expenses", Staff see their own expenses only, as Field do. Migration `0123` opens every section for every existing Team; Field's Jobs and Meetings are fixed (`FIELD_ALWAYS_CAPABILITIES`).
- **Closed, not in the way (US-TEAM-31):** pickers answer `?picker=1` without the Open permission, with only what forms need (contacts and a contact's addresses, catalogue items and bundles, jobs, invoices for payments and credit notes, bills for vendor payments and credits). A record in a closed section shows as plain text with a lock (`SectionLink`, 60-odd links); search, Needs your attention, the activity feed and dashboard cards leave closed sections out.
- **Team page (US-TEAM-32):** a "Sections" card above the groups with a switch and Can work here / View only / Can't open per section, in the same draft; a closed section's actions read "Open <section> first." and can't be switched; the save confirm says "They'll see" / "They'll no longer see <section>". Copy no longer says reading needs no permission.
- **Tests:** API integration `us-team-30-31` (with the Open permissions added to existing tests' Teams); web unit `us-team-30-32` (and `us-team-25-29` updated); E2E `us-team-30-32` (with axe). QA script section 232.

Reading records never needs a permission, so a section in a Staff member's sidebar with nothing allowed there is view only — it used to read as open.

The dialog suggests a strong password (`generatePassword` in `@spine/shared`), shown to pass on with Copy and Hide, or takes a typed one, with the sign-up strength meter and what happens. `PUT /org/members/:id/password` ends every session of theirs, sets `user.must_change_password` (migration `0120`), emails `password_set_by_owner` (never the password; logged as not sent without SMTP) and audits `password_set` without the password.

The web app sends them to `/choose-password` (layout redirect and the API client); the field app shows its own screen through `Stack.Protected`, and a reset by email link counts as choosing.

### 2026-10-12 — Expense categories that guide (US-PUR-46 to US-PUR-48, US-ONB-32, D-228)

- **A table that shows use (US-PUR-46):** Expenses › Categories is a table on the layout every refined list shares — each category's name over what it's for, the account it posts to ("6900 Other Expenses · default" when none is set, the account its expenses already fall back to), its default tax rate, how many expenses use it (a link to the expenses list filtered to it), what was approved in it this month, and Active or Archived in words; the 5200 category is tagged "Headcount × day rate". State filter and search in the address, Refresh, empty states. The figures come from `GET /expense-categories/usage`, for Owners and Staff only.
- **Safe changes (US-PUR-47):** one form for adding and editing with "What it's for" (up to 120 characters) and a default tax rate; a note when the account changes saying approved expenses stay where they posted; names are unique ignoring case and spaces, archived included (409 naming the one that has it, with Restore for an archived one); the row menu gains icons, View history and Delete (only while unused — it existed in the API but wasn't offered); Archive and Delete ask first; every change toasts. Migration `0116` adds `description` and `default_tax_rate_id` and renames existing duplicate names "<name> (2)" before the unique index.
- **Picking one (US-PUR-48):** the expense form's category picker shows each category's line and searches it; choosing a category with a default rate fills in the tax rate (and works the tax out), never over a rate the person chose or on a saved expense. `Combobox` options can carry a description line (docs and a11y test updated).
- **Templates and demo (US-ONB-32):** every industry template's categories come with their line; a demo loaded into an empty business defaults the goods categories to the country's single expense rate (VAT/GST countries other than India), and every demo adds an archived "Client entertainment"; deleting the demo undoes both.
- **Tests:** API integration `us-pur-46-48` and the US-ONB-32 checks in `us-onb-24-28`; web unit `us-pur-46-48`; E2E `us-pur-46-48`, with `us-pur-19`, `us-onb-13` and `us-onb-20` moved to the table. QA script section 222.

### 2026-10-11 — Demo expenses with their receipts (US-ONB-31, D-216)

- **Receipts:** every demo expense has a receipt PDF printed for it — a till roll from a shop that fits what it was for, in the business's town, with the date, what was bought, the tax inside the amount and the total — stored like any upload and shown on the expense's page. Riverstone's "Fuel — no receipt attached" still has none. Without file storage the demo loads without receipts.
- **Their days:** a demo expense is submitted on its date and approved or rejected the day after, so its Activity reads like a real one's.
- **Removing it:** "Delete all demo data" removes the receipts of the demo expenses it deletes.
- **Tests:** `us-onb-24-28` checks the receipts, their file type and access, the Activity days and that the receipts go with the demo. QA script section 221.

### 2026-10-11 — Expenses you can work from (US-PUR-42 to US-PUR-45, D-227)

- **A list that shows money (US-PUR-42):** cards Waiting for approval (a Staff approver's counts other people's; an Owner's counts all), Approved this month, Ready to bill and Rejected, in the business's currency; rows with the date, the number once approved, description over category, job, who submitted it, who paid, the state in words (Billed worked out from the invoice line, never stored) with Billable, and the amount, newest first; the list layout every refined list shares, with state, category, submitted by, job, card, search, sort and page in the address and Refresh; a row menu with icons (View, Edit or Fix and resubmit, Approve, Reject, Bill to customer, Delete). `GET /expenses` gains `submittedByName`, `reviewerName`, `jobName` and the invoice it was billed on.
- **An expense's own page (US-PUR-43):** `/expenses/:id` replaces the drawer (old `?id=` links open it): the state in words, who and what, Reject / Approve and next / Approve for an approver, Bill to customer when ready, a strip of Amount, Tax, Paid from and Billable, the receipt previewed beside the details, Attachments and Activity (`GET /expenses/:id/activity`). Approve and reject now record the comment in the audit trail.
- **Submitting faster (US-PUR-44):** a currency picker; tax worked out as included in the amount from the rate until typed over; a warning when you already submitted the same amount on the same day (`GET /expenses/possible-duplicates`); several files at once ("Add files" or drop) — the first is the receipt, the rest are attached — in a files pane that scrolls on its own; "Submit and add another".
- **Fix and resubmit (US-PUR-45):** editing a rejected expense resubmits it (waiting again, the review cleared, "Fixed and resubmitted" in Activity); an approved expense can no longer be edited, since it has posted.
- **Icons (D-222):** Approve — `Check`; Reject — `X`.
- **Tests:** API integration `us-pur-42-45` and `us-pur-10`; web unit `us-pur-42-45`; E2E `us-pur-42-45`, with `us-pur-10`, `us-pur-20`, `us-pur-22`, `us-pur-23`, `us-bank-03` and `us-ops-34` moved to the new list and page. QA script section 220.

### 2026-10-11 — Demo bills and vendor credits at every stage (US-ONB-30, D-216)

- **Bills:** every demo has a part-paid bill due within the week (Riverstone's AT-20441, 40% paid) and a second draft, so Due in 7 days, Partly paid and "Record (2)" show something. Industry demos gain a draft from the services vendor.
- **Vendor credits:** industry demos have four — partly used against the overdue bill, unused for about 70 days, used up by a refund, and a draft; Riverstone has two (partly used against GSC-8834, and a draft). They're entered, recorded, applied and refunded through the real services on their own days, with Activity on those days.
- **Recurring bills:** every demo has one active monthly schedule starting in a few days (Riverstone: Ridgeline "Equipment yard rental").
- **Removing it:** `vendor_credits`, `vendor_refunds` and `recurring_bills` gain `is_demo` (migration `0115`), set only for what the demo loads; a demo credit's ledger entries are demo entries; "Delete all demo data" removes demo credits with their applications and refunds, and the demo schedule. A credit or schedule a person records for a demo vendor stays theirs and keeps that vendor (D-191).
- **Tests:** `us-onb-24-28` checks bills, credits and the schedule for Riverstone, India construction and UK IT services, and that deleting the demo removes them. QA script section 219.

### 2026-10-11 — Vendor credits you can work from (US-PUR-37 to US-PUR-41, D-226)

- **A credit's page (US-PUR-37):** the credit number as the title ("Draft vendor credit" before it's recorded) with its state in words — Draft, Open, Partly used, Used, Void (Partly used worked out from what's left, never stored) — the vendor, their credit note number, the date, the bill it's against, job and place of supply in the business's formats; one primary action (Record credit, or Apply to bills while credit is left) with Record refund beside it; View history, Edit, Void credit and Delete draft with their icons; a strip of Total, Applied to bills, Refunded and Left to use ("—" when void); lines with their tax, account and HSN/SAC; the bills it was applied to and its refunds, each removable from its row menu after asking; attachments; Activity (`GET /vendor-credits/:id/activity`: created, recorded, applied, removed from a bill, refunded, refund deleted, voided with the reason). Every action now says what happened in a toast, and "Delete draft" asks first (it deleted at once). `GET /vendor-credits/:id` gains `jobName` and each line's `accountCode` and `accountName`; removing a credit from a bill now records which bill.
- **A list that shows money (US-PUR-38):** cards Credit to use, Drafts to record, Partly used and Unused 60+ days (the last three filter), in the business's currency (the "to use" figure took the first credit's currency); rows with their number, the bill it's against, job, the state in words and "—" for a void credit; newest by date first (it was by entry); the list layout every refined list shares, with state, vendor, job, card, search, sort and page in the address and Refresh; a row menu with icons (View, Edit, Record, Apply to bills, Record refund, Void, Delete); the title is "Vendor credits". `GET /vendor-credits` gains `billNumber` and `jobName`.
- **Entering a credit like a bill (US-PUR-39):** the new and edit credit pages use the bill builder (header card, compact item rows with Account, the bar that stays at the bottom, "Leave without saving?"); a warning when the vendor already has a non-void credit with the same credit note number, ignoring case and spaces (`GET /vendor-credits/possible-duplicates`), which still lets you save; choosing "Against bill" asks before replacing lines already entered; "Save and record" (asks first).
- **From a bill (US-PUR-40):** a recorded bill's More offers "Record vendor credit" (`/vendor-credits/new?billId=`), filled from the bill; applying a credit puts the bill it's against first and fills it first, shows each bill's due date and how late, and reads what's applied and what's left as the amounts change.
- **Record several drafts (US-PUR-41):** tick credits, "Record (n)" counts the drafts, asks first with their total, records each on its own and names any skipped (`POST /vendor-credits/bulk/record`).
- **Also:** a duplicated bill keeps its own currency (it took the business's).
- **Tests:** API integration `us-pur-37-41`; web unit `us-pur-37-41`; E2E `us-pur-37-41`, with `us-pur-24` to `us-pur-28` moved to the new page and builder. QA script section 218.

### 2026-10-11 — Vendor bills you can work from (US-PUR-32 to US-PUR-36, D-225)

- **A bill's page (US-PUR-32):** the bill number as the title with its state in words — Draft, Unpaid, Partly paid, Paid, Overdue, Void (worked out, never stored) — the vendor, bill date, due date with how late, job and place of supply in the business's formats; one primary action (Record bill, or Pay bill while money is owed); Edit, View history, Make recurring, Duplicate, Void bill and Delete draft with their icons; a strip of Total, Paid, Credits applied and Balance due ("—" when void); lines with their totals; Payments; credits; attachments; Activity (`GET /vendor-bills/:id/activity`: created, recorded, paid, credit applied, voided with the reason). Voiding has its own dialog that needs a reason.
- **Pay from the bill (US-PUR-33):** "Pay bill" opens the vendor payment dialog with the bill ticked for its balance (`initialBillId`) — before, "Record payment" only opened the vendor payments list; "Apply credit" (while the vendor has open credit) fills their credits oldest first up to the balance and applies them; each payment row opens that payment.
- **A list that shows money (US-PUR-34):** cards To pay, Overdue, Due in 7 days and Drafts to record (the last three filter); rows with the bill date, due date and how late, job, the state in words, and "—" for a void bill's balance (it showed the full amount); newest bill first; the list layout every refined list shares, with state, vendor, job, card, search, sort and page in the address and Refresh; a row menu with icons (View, Edit, Record, Pay bill, Duplicate, Void, Delete). `GET /vendor-bills` and `/:id` gain `jobName`.
- **Safer entry (US-PUR-35):** the new and edit bill pages use the invoice and quote builder layout (header card, compact rows with an Account column, item lines only, the bar that stays at the bottom, due quick picks, "Leave without saving?"); a warning when the vendor already has a non-void bill with the same number, ignoring case and spaces (`GET /vendor-bills/possible-duplicates`), which still lets you save; the due date suggested from the vendor's last bill; "Save and record" (asks first); "Duplicate" opens a new bill filled from another (`/vendor-bills/new?from=`).

### 2026-10-11 — Receivables you can chase from (US-PAY-27 to US-PAY-31, D-224)

- **By customer (US-PAY-27):** Receivables opens on "By customer" — one row per customer who owes, with their open invoices, how late the oldest is, overdue, total owed, credit to use (unapplied payments and credit notes) and the last reminder, most overdue money first, with a totals row; each row opens onto that customer's invoices. "By invoice" is the list as before. `GET /reports/receivables` gains `customers`.
- **Act from the page (US-PAY-28):** an invoice's menu offers Record payment (that invoice ticked), Apply credit (when the customer has credit), Send reminder (or why it can't go), Download PDF and Open invoice; a customer's offers Send statement, Record payment and Open contact — all through the existing dialogs, each asking first and toasting when done. Ticked invoices get "Send reminders (n)", which names what it skips and why.

CSV, Excel and PDF follow the age (`age=` on the report and its CSV). This finishes US-PAY-09's "click a bucket".

Invoice rows gain `issueDate`, `type`, `paidAmount`, `jobId`, `jobName`, `lastReminderOn`, `remindedToday` and `contactHasEmail`.
- **Trust the number (US-PAY-31):** "as of" the day in the header; retention held on jobs as a note under the bar with "See jobs" (`retention` in the report); Updated and Refresh; tab, age, search, sort and page in the address. The page uses the list layout every refined list shares instead of the report toolbar's "Show".
