### 2026-10-06 — Fix: Escape didn't close a drawer or dialog while a toast showed

- Radix treats an open toast as the topmost dismissable layer, so the first Escape only dismissed the toast, even with focus in a drawer or dialog. This affected every drawer and dialog that shows a toast.
- `Toast` in `@spine/ui` now passes Escape on when focus isn't on the toast. The toast closes, and once it's gone the same Escape reaches the dialog or drawer underneath, so one press closes both. With focus on the toast, Escape still closes only the toast. The design-system Toast page's keyboard table says so.
- The expense form, opened with "Edit" from a drawer's menu, used to drop focus onto the page when it closed, because the menu item that opened it was gone. Focus now returns to the drawer's "More" (or "Edit") button. `ExpenseForm` gains `returnFocusTo`.

Tests:
- `packages/ui/src/components/toast.a11y.test.tsx`: Escape with a toast and a dialog open closes both. It fails without the fix.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-pur-10.spec.ts`: after saving an edit, focus is on "More actions", and one Escape closes the drawer while the toast still shows.

### 2026-10-06 — Fix: "Edit" was missing on submitted expenses (US-PUR-10)

- US-PUR-10 was ticked in Phase 4. The API (`PUT /expenses/:id`) and the form's edit mode existed, but nothing in the web app ever opened the form on an existing expense. Now:
  - The expense drawer has "Edit" while an expense is "Submitted" and you can edit it: your own with `expenses.edit_own` (Field always), anyone's with `expenses.edit_others`. It's a button when there's no review to make; when Approve and Reject show, it's under "More". Saving shows "Expense updated", and the expense stays "Submitted".
  - The Expenses list's row menu has "Edit" for submitted expenses.
- Editing an approved or rejected expense through the API now returns the story's message: "Only a submitted expense can be edited — an approved expense has already posted to the ledger."
- The expense form's "Amount" and "Tax amount" fields are now linked to their labels, so screen readers announce them.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/purchases/us-pur-10.http.integration.test.ts` (3):
  - An edit stays submitted, and a delete works.
  - Approved and rejected expenses are refused, with the message.
  - Staff without "edit others'" get 403. Field can edit and delete their own and are refused on others'. The Owner can edit anyone's.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-pur-10.spec.ts` (1): edit from the drawer; Edit in the row menu; Edit under "More" on a colleague's expense; no Edit on an approved one.
- `us-pur-23.spec.ts` matches "Amount" exactly, now that "Tax amount" is labelled.

### 2026-10-06 — Credit note, vendor payment and expense drawers match the payment drawer (Phase 8)

- The credit note, vendor payment and expense drawers are now `size="lg"`. Their footers follow the payment drawer's pattern: History, then the main action, then a "More" menu (shown only when it has something in it), then "Close". The footer wraps at narrow widths.
  - **Credit note:** "Refund" is the main action when there's credit left and you have the right. "Download PDF" (or "Preview PDF" for a draft) goes under "More". With nothing to refund, the PDF is the main button.
  - **Vendor payment:** "Delete payment" moves under "More".
  - **Expense:** "Reject" and "Approve" stay side by side, and so does "Bill to customer". "Delete expense" moves under "More".

Tests:
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-pur-20.spec.ts` finds "Delete expense" under "More actions". The full e2e suite passes (190).

The menu holds "Download PDF", "Email receipt" (when it isn't the main action), "Refund" and "Delete payment" (destructive). The footer wraps at narrow widths.

  - The top row shows "Tax collected" for the period, "Input tax available" and "Net payable".
  - The fields are "Period" (the report's range, last quarter or last month), "Date", "Input tax used", "Amount paid", "Paid from" (last used preselected), "Reference" and a posting preview.
  - "Input tax used" defaults to the smaller of the input tax available and the tax collected, and "Amount paid" defaults to the net payable.
  - Using more than is available shows "You have <amount> of input tax to use. Lower the amount."
  - "Record a tax refund instead" switches the dialog to a refund: Amount, Paid into, Date and Reference.
- **Posting.**
  - A payment posts P26: Dr 2100 (paid + input tax used) / Cr 1200 (input tax used) and Cr the money account (paid).
  - A refund posts P27: Dr the money account / Cr 1200.
  - Neither can take more from 1200 than it holds on the date (D-175, new). The Tax Summary leaves these entries out, so its figures don't change.
- **"Tax payments and refunds"** below the Tax Summary lists those paid in the range or covering it. "Delete" posts the reversal dated today, and the row stays, struck through. In money account registers the entries read "Tax payment", "Tax refund", and "Tax payment deleted" or "Tax refund deleted".
- **Migration 0088**: `tax_payments`.
- **API.** `GET/POST /tax-payments`, `GET /tax-payments/input-tax-available`, `DELETE /tax-payments/:id` and `POST /tax-refunds`, all Owner-only. Staff (whatever their Team grants) and Field users get 403.
- Known gap until US-BANK-07: deletes aren't yet blocked once reconciled, because reconciliation doesn't exist yet.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/tax/us-tax-08.http.integration.test.ts` (5):
  - Too much input tax is refused, with the formatted message.
  - P26 lines, the Tax Summary unchanged, the list, the register label and the audit row.
  - P27 within the input tax left, and over it refused.
  - Delete reverses and returns the input tax; deleting twice is refused; the register shows both rows.
  - Staff and Field get 403 on every route.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-tax-08.spec.ts` (1):
  - The dialog's figures and defaults, the over-limit message and recording.
  - The list and delete.
  - A refund via "Record a tax refund instead".

### 2026-10-06 — US-PUR-28: let a Team manage vendor credits (Phase 8)

- **"Vendor credits"** in the privilege matrix: "Create/edit own", "Edit others'", "Delete own draft", "Delete others' draft", "Record", "Apply and refund" and "Void vendor credits". All seven are granted to General Staff, and existing Teams were backfilled from their bill rights (migration 0085, US-PUR-24). Without "Record vendor credits" a draft has no "Record" and the API returns 403. Field users get 403 on every vendor credit route.

Tests:

- `apps/api/src/modules/purchases/us-pur-28.http.integration.test.ts` (3): General Staff hold all seven; recording returns 403 without the right and 201 with it; Field gets 403 on all twelve routes.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-pur-28.spec.ts` (1): the matrix group's seven switches are on; a Staff member without "Record" sees "Edit" but no "Record".

### 2026-10-06 — US-PUR-27: void a vendor credit (Phase 8)

- **"Void"** on an open or used vendor credit asks for a reason, then posts P24's reversal (dated today) and marks it "Void — <reason>"; the list shows "Void" with a zero balance. With applications or refunds it refuses: "Remove its <n> applications and refunds first.". Drafts are deleted instead.
- **API.** `POST /vendor-credits/:id/void { reason }` (`vendor_credits.void`).

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/purchases/us-pur-27.http.integration.test.ts` (3): the reversal lines and "Void"; refused with an application and a refund; reason required and 403 without the right.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-pur-27.spec.ts` (1): refused with a refund, then voided after deleting it.

### 2026-10-06 — US-PUR-26: record a refund from a vendor (Phase 8)

- **"Record refund"** on an open vendor credit (`VendorRefundDialog`): "Amount" (up to the balance, prefilled with it), "Date", "Paid into" (money account), "Method" and "Reference". Over the balance: "This credit has <amount> left to refund.".
- **Posting** (P25): Dr the money account / Cr 2000 AP, contact-tagged; the credit's balance drops, "Used" at zero. The register reads "Vendor refund".
- **Delete**: "Delete refund" in "Refunds from the vendor" posts the reversal dated today and gives the balance back.
- **Database limit**: the deferred trigger now counts live refunds with applications, so together they never exceed the credit (invariant 9).
- **Migration 0087**: `vendor_refunds` and the trigger update.
- **API.** `POST /vendor-credits/:id/refunds`, `DELETE /vendor-refunds/:id` (`vendor_credits.apply`).

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/purchases/us-pur-26.http.integration.test.ts` (4): over the balance refused; P25 lines, balance and Used, the register's rows; deleting reverses and returns the balance; the database refuses refunds beyond the credit.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-pur-26.spec.ts` (1): the dialog's limit message, recording into Bank Account, and deleting the refund.

### 2026-10-06 — US-PUR-25: apply a vendor credit to bills (Phase 8)

- **"Apply to bills"** on an open vendor credit (`ApplyVendorCreditDialog`): the vendor's open bills oldest first, with "Amount to apply" prefilled oldest first from the credit's balance; "You can apply up to <amount> to this bill." over a bill's balance.
- **Nothing posts** (P24a): the credit and the bills already sit in 2000 AP. Each bill's new `credited_amount` and the credit's balance move; at zero the credit is "Used". "Applied to bills" on the credit lists each, with "Remove" to undo one.
- **On the bill**: "Credits applied" lists each vendor credit ("VCR-0001 · their CN-4471") and the totals show it. Every bill balance — the bill list and page, payables, the dashboard's attention items, search, and vendor payment checks — is now total − paid − credited. A bill with credits can't be voided until they're removed.
- **Pay bill**: when the vendor has open credit, the payment dialog says "<Vendor> has <amount> of credit. Apply it first?" with a link.
- **Database limits** (invariant 9): a deferred trigger keeps a credit's applications within its total and a bill's payments plus credits within its total; the vendor-payment trigger counts credits too; bills check `amount_paid + credited_amount <= total_amount`.
- **Migration 0086**: `vendor_credit_allocations`, `vendor_bills.credited_amount`, the triggers.
- **API.** `POST /vendor-credits/:id/apply`, `DELETE /vendor-credits/:id/allocations/:allocationId` (`vendor_credits.apply`).

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/purchases/us-pur-25.http.integration.test.ts` (5): limits on the bill, the credit and another vendor's bills; applying across bills posts nothing and marks the credit Used, the bill lists it; payments, voids and payables respect credits; removing an application; the database refuses credits beyond a bill.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-pur-25.spec.ts` (1): the pay-bill note, applying oldest first with the over-balance message, and the bill's "Credits applied".

### 2026-10-06 — US-PUR-24: record a credit from a vendor (Phase 8)

- **Purchases › Vendor Credits** (new): a list with Total, Draft, Open ("<amount> to use") and Used cards, status and vendor filters, and Number, Vendor's number, Vendor, Status (Draft, Open, Used, Void), Date, Amount and Balance.
- **New vendor credit** (`VendorCreditBuilder`): "Vendor", "Vendor's credit note number", "Against bill" (one of the vendor's recorded bills; its lines and job are copied in to edit down), "Currency", "Date", "Job", lines like a bill's, notes and attachments. Saves as a draft that can be edited or deleted.
- **Record** (D-141): numbers it `VCR-0001` and posts P24 — Dr 2000 AP for the total; Cr each line's account (6900 when a line has none) and 1200 Input Tax — contact-tagged and tagged with the job, so the job's cost drops. The credit shows "Open — <amount> to use" and can't be edited after; mistakes are voided (US-PUR-27).
- **Rights**: seven `vendor_credits.*` capabilities (edit own/others, delete own/others, record, apply, void), granted to General Staff by default and backfilled from each Team's matching bill rights; Field never (US-PUR-28 covers the matrix).
- Vendor credits can hold attachments. Settings › Numbering lists "Vendor Credit" (VCR).
- **Migration 0085**: `vendor_credits`, `vendor_credit_lines`, the `vendor_credit_status` enum and the capability backfill.
- **API.** `GET/POST /vendor-credits`, `GET/PUT/DELETE /vendor-credits/:id`, `GET /vendor-credits/:id/history`, `POST /vendor-credits/:id/record`.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/purchases/us-pur-24.http.integration.test.ts` (4): recording numbers VCR-0001 and posts P24 with the job and contact on every line, then locks the credit; "against bill" only the vendor's recorded bills; the list's fields and status filter; drafting without the record right and Field refused.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-pur-24.spec.ts` (1): a credit against a bill copying its lines, saved, recorded and listed.

- **"Refund customers"** (`payments.refund`) in the privilege matrix's "Manage Payments" group, off for General Staff and every existing Team (migration 0084, US-PAY-17). Without it, "Refund" is hidden on payments, credit notes and contacts, and `POST`/`DELETE /customer-refunds` return 403; Field always gets 403.

### 2026-10-06 — US-PAY-17: refund a customer (Phase 8)

- **"Refund"** on a payment with unallocated credit, a credit note with credit left (US-PAY-16), and a contact holding credit. `CustomerRefundDialog` asks for "Refund from", "Amount" (up to the credit), "Date", "Paid from" (money account, last used preselected), "Method", "Reference" and "Send refund confirmation". The toast reads "Refunded <amount> to <customer>".
- **Posting** (P23): Dr 2200 Customer Advances / Cr the money account, both contact-tagged. The source's credit drops in the same transaction, under a lock on it, and database checks keep refunds within it (`payments.refunded_amount`, `credit_notes.credit_remaining_amount`).
- **Confirmation email**: "We've refunded <amount>", receipt-style. If the customer has no email, email isn't set up, or the send fails, the refund is still saved and the toast says why.
- **Delete**: "Delete refund" in the source's "Refunds" list posts the reversal dated today and gives the credit back. A payment with refunds can't be deleted until they are.
- **Elsewhere**: payments' unallocated credit and "Apply credit" are net of refunds; registers read "Refund" / "Refund deleted"; statements show refunds as charge lines.
- **Migration 0084**: `customer_refunds`, `payments.refunded_amount`, and the `payments.refund` right (off for every Team; US-PAY-18 covers the matrix).
- **API.** `GET/POST /customer-refunds`, `DELETE /customer-refunds/:id`.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/payments/us-pay-17.http.integration.test.ts` (6): a partial refund posts P23, lowers the credit and sends the confirmation; over the credit refused and applying refunded credit refused; deleting reverses and returns the credit, and blocks deleting the payment first; a credit note's excess refunded; refunds and removals on the statement; 403 without `payments.refund`.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-pay-17.spec.ts` (1): the dialog from a payment, the toast and email, the refunds list, deleting a refund, and "Refund" on the contact.

### 2026-10-06 — US-PAY-16: keep a credit note's extra as customer credit (Phase 8)

- **Crediting past the balance** (D-142): "Issue credit note" is offered on paid invoices too. When the credit is more than what's left to pay, the dialog reads "<balance> reduces what's owed on <invoice>; <excess> becomes credit for <customer>.". P8 credits 1100 AR up to the balance and 2200 Customer Advances for the rest, kept on the credit note as `creditRemainingAmount` ("Credit for the customer" in its drawer). All credit notes on one invoice can't exceed its total.
- **Apply credit on an invoice**: a new "Apply credit" dialog on an unpaid invoice lists the customer's credit from unallocated payments and credit notes side by side, prefilled oldest first up to what's owed. A credit note's credit posts P6 (Dr 2200 / Cr 1100, tagged with the invoice's job) and is recorded in `credit_note_allocations`.
- **Statements** now read 1100 and 2200 together, so a credit note's excess shows as a credit line, applying credit nets out and isn't listed, and the balance due is what's owed less credit held. Customers without credit see no change.
- **Known edge**: a job's "billed to date" counts only the AR part of a credit note against its invoice; the excess lowers the job's revenue but not its billed figure.
- **Migration 0083**: `credit_notes.credit_remaining_amount` (never negative) and `credit_note_allocations`.
- **API.** `POST /credit-notes/:id/apply-credit` (`payments.apply_credit`); credit notes carry `creditRemainingAmount`.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/credit-notes/us-pay-16.http.integration.test.ts` (4): a paid invoice's credit all to 2200; a split between 1100 and 2200 and the cap at the invoice total; applying credit (P6) with its limits, another customer's invoice refused, 403 without the right; the statement's credit lines and balance.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-pay-16.spec.ts` (1): the split message on a paid invoice, then applying the credit to another invoice.

### 2026-10-06 — US-OPS-35: remove an attachment added by mistake (Phase 8)

- **"Remove"** on each file. On a record that can still be edited (a draft, a submitted expense, a contact), anyone who can edit it removes the file after a plain confirm.
- **Locked records** (sent quotes, issued invoices and credit notes, recorded bills and payments, reviewed expenses, bank entries): Staff and Field get no "Remove". An Owner removes with a required reason: "This bill is recorded, so only an owner can remove its files, with a reason. The bill's history records it.". The API refuses Staff with 403 and an Owner without a reason with 400.
- **History** reads "<name> removed attachment <file> — <reason>". The attachment row keeps who removed it, when and why.
- **Storage**: the stored file is deleted once nothing else uses it; one that is also a receipt or another record's file stays.
- **New bill**: a file dropped but not yet saved is simply taken off the list.
- **Migration 0082**: `attachments.file_id` becomes nullable, set null when its file is deleted.
- **API.** `DELETE /attachments/:id { reason? }`; the web client's `apiClient.delete` can now send a body.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/attachments/us-ops-35.http.integration.test.ts` (4): a draft's file removed without a reason and deleted from storage, audited; a recorded bill refused for Staff, and for an Owner without a reason, then removed with one and the reason kept; a file still used as a receipt stays; Field on their submitted expense but not an approved one.
- `packages/shared/src/us-ops-35.test.ts` (1): the history sentence with the reason.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-ops-35.spec.ts` (1): the plain confirm on a draft; no Remove for Staff on a recorded bill; the Owner's reason dialog and the history line.

### 2026-10-06 — US-OPS-34: open attachments only when I can open the record (Phase 8)

- **`GET /files/:id`** finds a file's owning record through `attachments` too (D-117) and applies that record's read rule, the same rule `GET /attachments` uses: Owners every record; Staff every record except a bank entry without `banking.view`; Field only their own expenses, never money documents. Anyone else, another organisation included, gets 404. Tested for every record type that holds files today.
- **Preview and download**: images and PDFs open in the browser; `?download=1` saves them (presigned `s3` links too).
- **Data export with files**: attached files go under `attachments/<record type>/<record id>/<file id>-<name>`; the rest stay in `files/`. The export also gains `attachments`, `money_accounts`, `bank_transfers` and `bank_transactions`.
- The storage-integrity warning names attached files ("Attachment on a vendor bill: “…”").

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/attachments/us-ops-34.http.integration.test.ts` (5): Owners read all nine record types inline and as a download; Staff all but a bank entry, which needs `banking.view`; Field only their own expense; another organisation 404 on all; the export's attachments/ paths and new tables.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-ops-34.spec.ts` (1): an Owner previews an invoice's file; a Field user sees and opens their expense's file but gets 404 for the invoice's.

### 2026-10-06 — US-OPS-33: attach files to bills, invoices and other records (Phase 8)

- **Attachments** (D-156): an "Attachments" section with "Add files" and a drop zone on quotes, invoices, credit notes, payments, vendor bills, vendor payments, expenses and contacts (Attachments tab); a money in/out entry's register menu › "Attachments" opens it in a drawer. Each file shows its name, size, who added it and when, a thumbnail for images and a badge for PDFs, and opens in the browser or downloads. Refunds, vendor credits, journals and opening documents get the section when those stories ship.
- **New bill**: files dropped on the "New bill" form upload straight away and are attached when the bill is saved.
- **Limits**: 10 files a record ("A record can hold up to 10 files. Remove one first."), 10 MB each (`POST /files` now says "This file is over 10 MB. Upload a smaller copy, or split the PDF." instead of "File too large"), and the five types US-OPS-29 accepts.
- **Rules**: adding needs the record's edit right (Field: their own expenses); reading follows the record's read rule, and anyone else gets a 404. Only a file you uploaded can be attached, and to one record.
- **Lists**: Vendor Bills and Expenses show a paperclip with the count ("2 attachments" to screen readers).
- **History**: "<name> attached <file>" on the record.
- **Download**: `GET /files/:id?download=1` saves the file instead of showing it.
- **Accessibility fix in `@spine/ui`**: `AlertDescription` is now full-contrast `on-surface`. Its muted text measured 4.37:1 on the critical tint, caught by this story's axe check, so every error alert in the app was slightly below AA.
- **Migration 0081**: `attachments` and the `attachment_entity` enum.
- **API.** `GET /attachments`, `POST /attachments`; `attachmentCount` on `GET /vendor-bills` and `GET /expenses`.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/attachments/us-ops-33.http.integration.test.ts` (6): adding and listing with name, size and creator, audited; the 11th refused; type and size messages; only your own upload, attached once; edit right to add, read right to list, Field on their own expense, 404 for other records and types; list counts.
- `packages/shared/src/us-ops-33.test.ts` (2): the history sentence; file sizes.
- `packages/ui/src/components/us-ops-33.contrast.test.ts` (10): alert text on every tint in both modes.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-ops-33.spec.ts` (1): a PDF dropped on a new bill and attached on save, a photo with a thumbnail, the type message, axe, and the paperclip on the list.

### 2026-10-06 — US-BANK-12: let a Team see balances, record money and reconcile (Phase 8)

- **Privilege matrix › Banking**: "View bank balances and registers" (`banking.view`), "Record transfers and other money in or out" (`banking.record`) and "Reconcile accounts" (`banking.reconcile`), off for General Staff (migration 0077 added them to every Team, off).
- **Reconciling needs viewing**: turning on "Reconcile accounts" turns on "View bank balances and registers"; turning viewing off turns reconciling off. The API applies it when privileges are saved (`withImpliedPrivileges`), and the matrix shows "“View bank balances and registers” turned on too. Reconciling needs to see the account.".
- **Without `banking.view`**: Banking leaves the sidebar, /banking shows the no-access page and the register API returns 403; payment forms still list account names, without balances.
- **Field** gets 403 on every banking route (tested route by route) and never sees Banking.
- **Accessibility**: every switch in the privilege matrix now has an accessible name (the privilege's label, or "Everything in <group>"); they had none.

Tests:
- `packages/shared/src/us-bank-12.test.ts` (2): the three rights, their labels and General Staff defaults; the implication both ways.
- `apps/api/src/modules/banking/us-bank-12.http.integration.test.ts` (5): General Staff start off; names-only pickers and a 403 register without `banking.view`; reconcile grants view and revoking view revokes reconcile; `banking.record` unlocks transfers; Field 403 on all twelve banking routes.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-bank-12.spec.ts` (2): the matrix group, both implications and their note; Staff without the right get the no-access page but keep the payment picker.

Otherwise it leaves the list, the totals and every picker (payments, vendor payments, expenses, transfers, money in/out), and keeps its register.
- **Archived accounts**: a new "Active accounts" / "Archived accounts" filter on Banking lists archived accounts; "Restore" brings one back unless an active account has taken its name in the meantime.

- **Money in / Money out** (`MoneyInOutDialog`, Banking): the account, "Date", "Category", "Amount", "Contact" (optional), "Tax" (money out only) and "Description". Shortcuts pick the usual categories: Owner's capital, Loan received and Interest received on money in; Owner's drawings, Loan repayment and Bank charges on money out (D-174).

Use a payment, bill or tax payment for this."; the API refuses them with the same message (and "Use a transfer between your accounts for this." for a money account).

### 2026-10-06 — US-BANK-04: move money between my accounts (Phase 8)

- **Transfer** (`TransferDialog`, Banking › "Transfer"): "From", "To", "Amount", "Date" and "Reference", with "Posts Dr <To> / Cr <From>. Both registers show it." and "Move money". The toast reads "Moved <amount> from <From> to <To>". Paying off a card is a transfer to the card.
- **Posting** (P22): Dr the destination's ledger account, Cr the source's, dated the transfer's date, in the same transaction as the `bank_transfers` row. A date in a closed period is refused ("… falls within a closed fiscal period"); the same account twice is refused with "Choose two different accounts.".

### 2026-10-06 — US-BANK-03: choose the account money went into or came out of (Phase 8)

- **"Paid into" / "Paid from"** on Record payment (Payments and an invoice), Record vendor payment (including "Pay bill"), the expense form and the approve dialog. It lists the organisation's active bank, cash and card accounts, and starts on the account this person used last on that kind of form ("Last used by you. Change it if the money went into another account."), else the organisation default.
- **Posting** (P4, P5, P5a, P13, P15; D-140): each posts to the chosen account's ledger account instead of 1010. An expense paid by card credits the card's liability account. No new posting rules.
- **Expenses**: Owners and Staff choose the account when they enter an expense; a Field user's expense starts on the default (Field never see accounts), and the approver can change it in the approve dialog before it posts. Bulk approve posts each expense to the account it carries.
- **Read-only afterwards**: a recorded payment, vendor payment or expense shows "Paid into" / "Paid from" beside its method.
- **Validation**: an archived account, or another organisation's, is refused with "Choose one of your active accounts.".
- **Migration 0078**: `money_account_id` on payments, vendor payments and expenses, backfilled from the account each one actually posted to (then the default for submitted expenses), and `user.last_money_accounts`.
- **API.** `moneyAccountId` on `POST /payments`, `POST /vendor-payments`, `POST/PUT /expenses` and `POST /expenses/:id/approve` (replaces the unused `bankAccountId`); detail responses carry `moneyAccount { id, name }`; new `GET /money-accounts/last-used`.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/banking/us-bank-03.http.integration.test.ts` (7): a payment posts to and carries the chosen account and is remembered; the default without a choice; archived and other organisations' accounts refused; a vendor payment from cash; an expense by card credits the card; a Field expense starts on the default and the approver changes it; Field can't list accounts.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-bank-03.spec.ts` (1): the default then last-used preselection, the read-only account on the payment, the register balance, and changing an expense's account while approving it.

### 2026-10-06 — US-BANK-02: see every account's balance and what moved through it (Phase 8)

- **Banking list**: each account's name opens its register. New "Reconciliation" ("Never reconciled", or "Reconciled" with the date) and "Unreconciled" ("<n> entries") columns, and "Not yet reconciled" and "Last reconciled" cards at the top.
- **Account register** (`/banking/<id>`): the title, bank, last four digits, type and ledger code; "Balance in Spine", "Money in" and "Money out" for the range, and "Unreconciled"; From and To dates (this month by default) and "All entries" / "Unreconciled only". One row per journal line on the account, newest first: Date, What ("Payment RCP-0018", "Vendor payment VP-0009", "Expense EXP-0031"), Contact, Reference, Money in, Money out, Balance and Status. The opening balance before the range is shown under the table, and every running balance starts from it, so filtering never changes the figures. Clicking "What" opens the payment, vendor payment or expense.
- **Cards** read "Paid off" and "Spent", and balances are what's owed.
- **Empty account**: "Nothing has gone in or out of this account yet."
- **Read from the ledger** (D-011): the register reads `journal_lines` on the account, never documents, and its balance matches the Balance Sheet for the same date (tested).
- **Reconciliation not built yet**: until US-BANK-08–US-BANK-10, every entry shows "Not reconciled", every account "Never reconciled", and the unreconciled count is every entry.
- **Vendor Payments** opens a payment from `?id=<payment>`, as Payments and Expenses do, so register rows can link to it.
- **API.** `GET /money-accounts/:id/register?from=&to=&unreconciled=` (`banking.view`); `GET /money-accounts` adds `lastReconciledOn` and `unreconciledCount` for callers with `banking.view`.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/banking/us-bank-02.http.integration.test.ts` (7): balances and unreconciled counts on the list; rows with what, contact, reference and running balance; a range starting from the true balance, with and without "unreconciled only"; matching the Balance Sheet on two dates; a card's spent, paid off and owed; an empty account; 403 without `banking.view` and no balances in the names-only list.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-bank-02.spec.ts` (1): the list's balance and reconciliation columns, the register row and filter, opening the payment from the register, the empty account and a card's columns.

### 2026-10-06 — US-BANK-01: add my bank, cash and card accounts (Phase 8)

- **Banking** (new "Money" section in the sidebar): every bank, cash and card account with its "Balance in Spine", a "Default" badge, and "Bank and cash" / "Owed on cards" totals. Owners add accounts with "Add account" and edit them from the row menu.
- **Add account** (`MoneyAccountDialog`): "Type" (Bank account, Cash, Credit card), "Name", "Bank name" and "Last 4 digits" (bank and card only), and "Use as default for new payments". The hint names the ledger account it will create ("Ledger account 1011 · Asset.").
- **Ledger accounts** (D-140): a bank or cash account takes the next free asset code from 1011 to 1099; a card takes the next free liability code from 2300 to 2399, and its balance is what's owed. Renaming an account renames its ledger account. A name already used by another active account (any case) is refused with "You already have an account called <name>. Choose another name.". Ticking default moves the badge (one default per organisation, enforced by a partial unique index).
- **Migration 0077**: new `money_accounts` table. 1000 Cash in Hand and 1010 Bank Account become money accounts in every existing organisation, 1010 the default; sign-up seeds the same two. Adds the three banking capabilities (`banking.view`, `banking.record`, `banking.reconcile`), off for every Team (US-BANK-12 finishes the privilege matrix).
- **Access**: adding and changing accounts is Owner-only (Staff get 403). Any Owner or Staff member can list account names for payment forms; balances need `banking.view`. Field gets 403 on every banking route, and Banking stays out of their sidebar.
- **Cash figures**: the dashboard's "Cash position" and "Money in and out" now sum every bank and cash account instead of the fixed 1000/1010 pair, so a new bank account counts. Cards aren't cash.
- **Audit log**: adding and changing an account are recorded as "Money account".
- **API.** `GET /money-accounts`, `GET /money-accounts/next-codes`, `POST /money-accounts`, `PUT /money-accounts/:id`.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/banking/us-bank-01.http.integration.test.ts` (8): the starting accounts; a bank account at 1011 with a zero balance, cash without bank details; a card at 2300 as a liability; duplicate names; moving the default; renaming renames the ledger account and is audited; Staff 403 on create and update; next codes.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-bank-01.spec.ts` (1): the starting list, the dialog's fields and hint, adding a bank account and a card, the duplicate-name message, and moving the default.

### 2026-10-05 — US-OPS-26: use every new screen in dark mode (Phase 7)

- **Sweep**: every Phase 7 screen is checked in light and dark mode, with a screenshot of each attached to the test report and axe (WCAG 2.1 A/AA, contrast included) passing in both. The screens are the Owner and Field dashboards, the search palette, invoices with bulk selection, Settings › Automatic reminders and Data export, the audit log, Activity, Profile › Notifications, recurring invoices, job costing and the job profitability report.
- **What the sweep caught and fixed**:
  - Light `due-soon` and `category-amber` text (`#9A6300`) was 4.46:1 on its tint. It's now `#946000` (4.72:1).
  - Dark muted text (`#93907F`) was 4.41:1 on sunken surfaces such as table headers and the palette footer. It's now `#9A9786` (4.83:1).
  - Both values are updated in `tokens.json`, the web preset, the native tokens and the design docs.
  - The setup checklist's checkboxes and the contact filter on Invoices, Quotes, Payments, Jobs and Credit notes had no accessible name. They're labelled now.
- **Overlays and elevation as tokens**: dialogs, alert dialogs, drawers and the search palette sit on a new `scrim` token, darkened to 62% in dark mode. Menus, popovers, dialogs and toasts use new `shadow-raised`, `shadow-overlay` and `shadow-toast` tokens, which are stronger in dark mode, so surfaces stay distinct from the page. These replace the raw `bg-black/30` and `rgba(...)` shadows in 16 `@spine/ui` components.
- **Dashboard**: money-out bars use `border-strong`, as in the dark design. The sidebar shows the light wordmark in dark mode (already in place; now tested).
- **Emails and PDFs**: emails declare a light colour scheme and carry only light values (tested). PDFs have no theme.
- **E2E**: a shared axe helper (`tests/axe.ts`) injects `axe-core` (new dev dependency, already used by `@spine/ui`).

Tests:
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-ops-26.spec.ts` (25): 12 screens in each theme (page colours from tokens, a screenshot, axe), plus dark-mode wordmark, scrim, palette surface and money-out bar colour.
- `apps/api/src/modules/email/us-ops-26.test.ts` (2): emails are light only.

### 2026-10-05 — US-OPS-23: use search with the keyboard and a screen reader (Phase 7)

- **Screen readers**: the palette is announced as a dialog named "Search". The field is a combobox controlling a listbox of options.
- **Keyboard**: the first result is highlighted as soon as results arrive. Up and Down move the highlight, and the active option is announced through `aria-activedescendant`, which the palette now keeps in step itself (`cmdk` only worked it out once, before late results existed, and its id lookup failed for values with ":"). Enter opens the highlighted record and closes the palette. Escape closes it and puts focus back where it was, even when it was opened with ⌘K and no button.
- **Reduced motion**: the palette appears at once with no scale-in. Otherwise it scales in over the base duration (`palette-in` keyframes in the shared theme).

Tests:
- `packages/ui/src/components/command-palette.a11y.test.tsx` (now 7): first result highlighted on arrival; Escape returns focus.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-ops-23.spec.ts` (2): roles and `aria-controls`; arrows move and announce the highlight; Enter opens and closes; Escape returns focus; no animation with reduced motion, `palette-in` without.

### 2026-10-05 — US-OPS-22: search everything from anywhere (Phase 7)

- **Search palette**: ⌘K (Mac) or Ctrl+K on any page, or the new search button in the sidebar footer beside Help and Notifications (the app has no top bar), opens it with focus in the field.
- **Results** from 2 characters: CONTACTS, QUOTES, JOBS, INVOICES, VENDOR BILLS and EXPENSES, up to 5 each. Each result has a second line ("Harborview Construction Co. · $9,700.00 due", "48 Harbor Way, Portland, ME 04101") and a status on the right ("Overdue", "Sent", "Active", "Contact"). Opening a result goes to the record.
- **Document numbers**: "INV-0010", "QUO-4" or "inv 10" puts that document first.
- **Nothing found**: "No results for “<query>”". The footer reads "↑↓ to move", "Enter to open" and "Searches contacts, quotes, invoices, jobs, bills and expenses".
- **Read rules**: results follow each module's own rules. Field users only see jobs they're on and their own expenses.
- **Speed**: Postgres trigram indexes (migration 0076, `pg_trgm`), no external service. Answers stay under 500 ms with 10,000 contacts.
- **`@spine/ui` `CommandPalette`** (new, on `cmdk`): grouped results with a second line and status, an Esc chip, a footer, and a scale-in only when motion is allowed. It has its design-system page and an a11y test.
- **API.** `GET /search?q=&limit=5`.

Tests:
- `packages/shared/src/us-ops-22.test.ts` (2): reading and matching document numbers.
- `apps/api/src/modules/search/us-ops-22.http.integration.test.ts` (5): groups, second lines and statuses; numbers first; Field's limits; the minimum length and no matches; under 500 ms at 10,000 contacts.
- `packages/ui/src/components/command-palette.a11y.test.tsx` (5): axe with results and empty; dialog, combobox and listbox roles; arrows and Enter; motion-safe only.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-ops-22.spec.ts` (1): shortcut and button, groups, number first, no results, opening a record, Field's limits.

### 2026-10-05 — US-SET-16: keep data exports private (Phase 7)

- **Owner-only link**: an export's download link answers 404 to everyone but an Owner of the organisation: Staff, Field, someone signed out, or an Owner of another organisation. It's never 401 or 403, so the link reveals nothing. The ZIP is never served through `/files/:id`, even to Owners, and the response isn't cached.
- **7-day expiry**: after 7 days the link answers 404 and the file is deleted from storage. That happens when someone uses the link or opens Settings › Data export, and otherwise on an hourly sweep (`data-export-cleanup`).
- **Audit log**: preparing and downloading an export appear as "Data export" entries with the person ("Prepared", "Downloaded").
- **The ready email** links to Settings › Data export only; the file is never attached or linked.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/data-export/us-set-16.http.integration.test.ts` (6): only Owners download (everyone else 404); never via `/files/:id`; audit entries with the person; the email's link; expiry deletes the file from storage; the hourly sweep.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-set-16.spec.ts` (1): the link for an Owner, Staff and a signed-out visitor; both actions in the audit log.

### 2026-10-05 — US-SET-15: export all my data (Phase 7)

- **Settings › Data export** (Owners): "Records as CSV" (on by default), "Records as JSON" and "Files", then "Prepare export". The button shows "Preparing…" and the pane says "Large exports are prepared in the background. We'll email you when the file is ready." It checks back every few seconds.
- **Last export**: "Oct 5, 2026 by Alex Morgan · 59.8 KB · Download again (link expires in 7 days)".
- **The ZIP** (`spine-export-<date>.zip`) contains:
  - one CSV with a header row (or JSON) per table, from contacts to journal lines; in `csv/` and `json/` folders when both are chosen;
  - `members` with names, emails and roles;
  - `files/` with receipts, photos, logos and issued PDFs when chosen;
  - a `README.txt` describing each file and its row count.

  It's built in the background (`data-export` queue) and streamed straight to private storage. Secrets are never exported: passwords, sessions, sign-in and quote tokens, push keys, and secret settings.
- **Email when ready**: "Your <Business> data export is ready", with a button to Settings › Data export (new `?settings=<pane>` link). The email never contains the file or a link to it.
- **Staff and Field** get 403 from `POST /org/exports`.
- **API.** `POST /org/exports`, `GET /org/exports`, `GET /org/exports/:id/download`. New `data_exports` table (migration 0075). Preparing an export is audit-logged.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/data-export/us-set-15.http.integration.test.ts` (5): no export yet; CSV ZIP with header rows, files and README; no secrets; JSON and both formats; Owner-only and audit-logged.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-set-15.spec.ts` (1): opened from the email link, defaults, prepared in the background, downloaded and unzipped; Staff refused.

### 2026-10-05 — US-NTF-13: get a daily digest by email (Phase 7)

- **One email each morning** at the member's "Send at", on their chosen days. The subject reads "Your Spine day: <n> things need you · Mon 5 Oct". The email opens "Good morning, Alex" and lists:
  - the dashboard's attention rows as counts, with totals for overdue invoices, expenses to approve and bills due ("1 · $4,542.50");
  - **Today**: meetings later today and milestones due within 7 days ("Due Sat · Mobilisation & site setup (Marina office fit-out)");
  - **Yesterday**: "$5,000.00 received · 1 quote accepted ($14,250.00) · 3 quotes viewed".

  It ends with "Open dashboard" and the footer "You get this daily digest because you're an owner of <business>. Turn it off or change the time in Profile › Notifications." ("a member of" for others).
- **Nothing to report, no email.** It goes out once a day at most, even if the worker runs late or twice. A run more than 3 hours after "Send at" skips the day. Without SMTP the worker logs and skips.
- **Field users** get counts only, with no money anywhere and no "Yesterday" section (D-048).
- **Defaults (D-093)**: on for Owners at 7:00 AM Monday to Friday, off for others (US-NTF-14 adds the controls).
- **Under the hood**: new `daily-digest` queue (every 15 minutes) and `DigestService`. New `user` columns `digest_enabled`, `digest_time`, `digest_days`, `digest_last_sent_on` (migration 0073). Money attention rows carry `total`. The email layout gains titled sections. `GET /meetings/upcoming`'s service takes a start time.

Tests:

- `apps/api/src/modules/dashboard/us-ntf-13.http.integration.test.ts` (5): not before "Send at"; the Owner's email content; once a day; Field without money; Staff off by default; nothing to report.

### 2026-10-05 — US-DASH-15: see money in and out by month (Phase 7)

- **"Money in and out"** replaces the revenue trend chart on the dashboard. Each month has a bar pair: "Money in (payments received)" and "Money out (bills, expenses)". The range is 3M, 6M or 12M (default 12M).
- **Totals above the chart**: "In · <range>", "Out · <range>" and "Net". Net is green with "+" when positive and red with "−" when negative. Hovering or focusing a month shows its in, out and net.
- **Empty state**: before any payment or bill, the card reads "Money in and out appears after your first payment or bill."
- **API.** `GET /reports/money-in-out?months=3|6|12` (`reports.view_dashboard_summary`) works from journal lines on the cash and bank accounts (1000, 1010), never from documents (D-011). A transfer between them counts as neither in nor out. The widget is in the layout under the key `money-in-out`, and Staff with the dashboard-summary grant see it too. `/reports/revenue-trend` is unchanged.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/reports/us-dash-15.http.integration.test.ts` (3): zero months before any activity; in, out, totals and net per range with a transfer ignored; Field refused.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-dash-15.spec.ts` (1): range default, totals, net sign, legend, month tooltip, switching to 3M.
- `apps/e2e/tests/dashboard.spec.ts`: widget heading and edit-layout checks now use "Money in and out".

### 2026-10-05 — US-DASH-14: see key figures with a trend and a comparison (Phase 7)

- **KPI row** (pinned, as before): "Cash position", "Profit this month", "Receivables" and "Overdue". Each card has the amount, a line of the last 8 weeks and "▲/▼ n% vs last month". The change is green when it's good for the business (cash and profit up; receivables and overdue down) and red otherwise, always with the arrow and sign. Receivables adds "Current … · overdue …"; Overdue adds "n invoice(s)". With nothing a month ago: "No data last month"; with no data at all: "No data yet".
- **Cards open their report**: cash → Balance Sheet, profit → P&L, receivables → Receivables, overdue → Receivables filtered to overdue (new `?overdue=1`, with "Show all"). Reports opens the tab named in `?tab=`.
- **API.** `GET /dashboard/kpis` works the figures out from journal lines (D-011), with overdue rebuilt from invoices, payments and credit notes as they stood on each date. It returns only the figures the member may see (US-DASH-17 adds the Staff set). The old summary card row is replaced.
- **Sparkline** (`@spine/ui`): a small decorative trend line in positive, negative and neutral tones, with its design-system page and a11y test.

Tests:

### 2026-10-05 — US-DASH-13: act on an attention item in one click (Phase 7)

- **Send reminder.** With one overdue invoice, the dashboard sends its reminder straight away; the toast reads "Reminder sent for INV-0002". With several, it opens Invoices filtered to overdue with those invoices ticked, ready for "Send reminders" (US-INV-23).
- **Other buttons go straight to the record:**
  - "Review": expenses filtered to submitted.
  - "Approve" and "Follow up": the quote.
  - "Raise invoice": the completed milestone's job.
  - "Pay bill": the vendor payment dialog, opened on that bill's vendor.
  - "Review" on a job over budget: its Costing tab.
- **No capability, no action.** Without the capability for an action (e.g. `invoices.send_reminder`), the button reads "Open" and just opens the record.
- **Deep links.** Invoices and Expenses read `?status=` (and Invoices `?select=overdue`). Vendor Payments reads `?contactId=&pay=1` to open "New vendor payment" on that vendor. No new endpoints.

Tests:
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-dash-13.spec.ts` (5): one-click reminder with toast; Review to submitted expenses; several overdue to the filtered, ticked list; Pay bill's payment dialog; "Open" without the capability.
- `apps/api/src/modules/dashboard/us-dash-12.http.integration.test.ts`: vendor bill rows carry the vendor.

### 2026-10-05 — US-DASH-12: see what needs my attention first (Phase 7)

- **Header.** The dashboard now opens with "Good morning|afternoon|evening, <first name>" and "<weekday> <day> <month> · <business> · <n> things need you today" (or "nothing needs you right now"), with "Edit layout" beside it (`DashboardOwnerRedesign`).
- **"Needs your attention"** comes first: one row per kind with an icon, a title with the count ("1 invoice is overdue"), a line naming the first record with its amount and age ("INV-0002 · Maple Ridge Developments · $4,542.50 · 12 days late") and an action button. The order is fixed: overdue invoices, expenses waiting for approval, quotes needing discount approval, milestones ready to invoice, quotes expiring within 7 days, vendor bills due within 3 days, jobs over budget. The chip beside the heading is the number of rows. It's rebuilt on every visit, so cleared items disappear on their own. With nothing open: "You're all caught up" (`DashboardCaughtUp`). For an Owner the setup checklist sits beside it until dismissed.
- **Server-side filtering.** `GET /dashboard/attention` leaves out kinds the member can't act on (e.g. no expense approvals without `expenses.approve`), never shows Staff their own expenses to approve (D-085), and shows Staff only their own expiring quotes. `describeAttention` (`@spine/shared`) writes each row. The buttons link to the matching page for now; US-DASH-13 makes them one-click.

- `apps/api/src/modules/dashboard/us-dash-12.http.integration.test.ts` (4): order, counts and first records; capability and ownership filtering; resolved items disappear; empty.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-dash-12.spec.ts` (2): header, chip and rows in order; "You're all caught up".

It shows three bars per job (contract value, billed to date, cost) for up to 10 jobs, largest contract first, with "Showing 10 of N jobs — the table lists them all." when there are more. A dashed marker over the cost bar shows the job's budget. The tooltip lists contract value, billed to date and %, cost, budget and % used, and margin.

- **Table** gains "Billed to date", "Billed %" and "Budget used" (with "Nearing" / "Over" text and icons); negative margins read in red. **CSV** gains the same columns as plain numbers, and its money columns are now decimals ("48500.00") like every other export, not minor units.
- **One source.** The report reads the same `billedByJob` / `costByJob` / `budgetFigures` as each job's Costing tab, so the figures always agree. The dashboard's job profitability widget keeps showing all jobs.
- **GroupedBarChart** (`@spine/ui`): grouped bars with a text legend, even ticks, a custom tooltip and an optional dashed marker over one series. It ships with its design-system page and a11y test. For screen readers the chart is a labelled image pointing to the table.

### 2026-10-05 — US-PUR-23: enter site labour as headcount × day rate (Phase 7)

- **"Enter as"** on "Submit an expense": "Amount" (as before, the default) or "Headcount × day rate" (`ExpenseDialogLabour`). The second swaps the Amount field for rows of Trade ("e.g. Mason"), Workers and Day rate, each with its total, "+ Add trade" and "Total for the day · N workers". Job becomes required ("Labour is always for a job.").
- **Helpful defaults.** A category posting to 5200 Direct Labour is preselected ("Preselected: posts to 5200 Direct Labour."). A trade used before is suggested and fills in its last day rate. A note says "Only headcounts and day rates are recorded — no names, attendance or hours." (D-126).
- **Checks.** An incomplete row: "Every row needs a trade, at least one worker and a day rate."; no job: "Labour is always for a job — choose one.". The server works out each row (headcount × day rate, exact) and refuses an amount that disagrees.
- **Posting** is unchanged: an approved labour expense posts like any expense to its category's account, tagged to the job. The expense drawer lists each row ("4 Mason × $950.00 = $3,800.00").
- **Data and API.** `expenses.entry_mode` and `expense_labour_lines` (migration `0072`); `entryMode` / `labourLines` on `POST/PUT /expenses`; `GET /expenses/labour-trades?q=`. Expense categories now include their posting account's code and name. Invalid expense bodies get a 400 with the reason instead of a server error. The phone form keeps the amount entry for now.

### 2026-10-05 — US-JOB-27: see how much of the contract has been billed (Phase 7)

- **Billing card** on the Costing tab: "Contract value", "Billed to date" with "40% of contract", "Left to bill" and a "Billed" bar. When billing passes the contract, "Left to bill" reads "Billed over contract by <amount>" in the danger colour, so a missing variation order gets noticed.
- **Jobs list** gains "Billed" (e.g. "40%") beside "Budget used", for job-profitability viewers.
- **From the ledger (D-127).** Billed to date is the net of 1100 AR and 1150 Retention Receivable lines tagged to the job from invoices, voids and credit notes. That makes it gross (tax included, before retention). Advance invoices post nothing at issue, so a deposit counts once a progress invoice offsets it; retention-release invoices are left out. `billingFigures` (`@spine/shared`) shares the maths.
- **Posting fix.** Credit notes now tag their journal lines with the credited invoice's job (P8 in doc 06), so a credit lowers the job's revenue and billed to date. Credit notes issued before this change aren't tagged; journal entries are immutable.

Tests:
- `packages/shared/src/us-job-27.test.ts` (3): 40% and left to bill, billed over, zero contract.
- `apps/api/src/modules/jobs/us-job-27.http.integration.test.ts` (6): nothing billed, gross progress invoices, advance and retention release excluded, credit note subtracted, billed over, list `billedPct`.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-job-27.spec.ts` (1): the card's figures and bar, the list column.

A donut sits beside it with the cost in the middle, showing the five largest accounts plus "Other" once there are more than six (the list always shows every account). ### 2026-10-05 — US-JOB-25: see budget spent and get warned before going over (Phase 7)

- **Costing tab** (`JobCostingBudget`) is laid out as cards: "Job costing" (contract value, revenue recognised, cost, margin, margin % — a negative margin reads "Cost is ahead of billing" in the danger colour) and a new **Budget** card. The Budget card shows "Cost budget", "Spent" and "Remaining", with a bar labelled "78% of budget used". From 80% it turns to the warning colour with an icon and "Nearing budget — 85% used". Past the budget it reads "Over budget by 1,450.00" with the danger colour and icon, "Remaining" goes negative and the bar is full. With no budget: "No budget set" and "Set a budget" (only with edit rights on the job), which opens Edit job. The old "cost tracking goes live later" note is gone.
- **Jobs list** gains "Budget used" ("78%", "Nearing · 85%", "Over · 104%", or "—") for members with `reports.view_job_profitability`, through `GET /jobs?include=costing`.
- **API.** `GET /jobs/:id/costing` gains `budget { amount, spent, remaining, usedPct, state }`, where spent is the job's cost from journal lines (D-011). The thresholds live in `budgetFigures` (`@spine/shared`), so every screen agrees.
- **Progress** (`@spine/ui`) gains `tone` (`default`, `warning`, `danger`, `neutral`), with its design-system page and a11y test.
- The dashboard's "job(s) over budget" attention row arrives with the attention list itself (US-DASH-12, step 5).

### 2026-10-05 — US-INV-23: send reminders for several invoices at once (Phase 7)

- **Select rows** on the Invoices list (a tick box per row). A bar appears: "<n> selected · <amount> outstanding", "Send reminders (<m>)" and "Clear" (`InvoicesBulkSelect`). The count includes only invoices that are issued, have a balance and weren't reminded today. Without `invoices.send_reminder` the button isn't shown.
- **Sending** reminds every eligible invoice's customer and toasts "3 reminders sent", listing each skipped invoice with its reason. Overdue isn't required: the email reads "is due in N days" or "is due today" as fits.
- **API.** `POST /invoices/bulk/send-reminders { ids }` (at most 100, D-097) returns `{ done, skipped }`. Each reminder sent counts as that day's reminder, so the automatic run and a second bulk send skip it. `GET /invoices` rows now carry `remindedToday`. Shared `bulkIdsSchema` / `bulkInvoiceIdsSchema` / `BulkResult` for the bulk stories.

### 2026-10-05 — US-INV-22: pause, edit or stop a recurring invoice (Phase 7)

- **Row menu** on Invoices › Recurring invoices: Edit (also by clicking the name), Pause / Resume and Delete.
- **Pause** stops the schedule creating invoices; "Next invoice" shows "—". **Resume** picks the next occurrence after today. Dates missed while paused aren't back-filled, and a schedule whose end passed while paused becomes Finished.
- **Edit** opens the same dialog with the schedule filled in ("Changes apply to invoices it creates from now on."). Invoices already created never change.
- **Delete** asks "Delete this schedule? Invoices it already created are kept." The invoices stay and lose their link to the schedule.
- **API.** `POST /recurring-invoices/:id/pause`, `/resume`, `DELETE /recurring-invoices/:id` (`invoices.manage_recurring`). History names "a recurring invoice" and the paused/resumed actions.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/invoices/us-inv-22.http.integration.test.ts` (5): list fields, paused creates nothing, resume to the next future date, editing leaves created invoices alone, delete keeps invoices.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-inv-22.spec.ts` (1): pause, resume, edit, delete with its confirmation.

### 2026-10-05 — US-INV-21: create recurring invoices automatically (Phase 7)

- **Run.** The `recurring-invoice-run` queue fires every 15 minutes. From 6:00 AM organisation time it gives each active schedule that's due one invoice, built from its lines with that day's tax (issue date today, due after the schedule's payment terms). It goes through the same create/issue/email code as a manual invoice, so the number, ledger posting and email are identical.
- **"Issue and email"** issues and emails the invoice. **"Create a draft"** leaves a draft, and Owners get "Recurring invoice ready to review: <name>" (in-app, email, push) linking to it. If issuing fails, the invoice stays a draft for an Owner to finish.
- **Never twice.** Invoices record `recurring_invoice_id` and `recurring_run_on`, unique together (migration `0068`), so a late, repeated or racing run can't make a second invoice for the same date. A run missed while the server was off catches up once; missed dates aren't back-filled.
- **Finished.** Once the end date or number of invoices is reached, the schedule shows "Finished" and has no next date.

Tests:
- `apps/api/src/modules/invoices/us-inv-21.http.integration.test.ts` (5): issue and email (number, posting, email, next date), draft plus notification, not before 6:00 and never twice (including a race), missed-run catch-up, finished by count and by date.

### 2026-10-05 — US-INV-20: set up a recurring invoice (Phase 7)

- **Invoices › Recurring invoices** (`RecurringInvoicesList`): name, customer, repeats ("Monthly · 1st", "Every 3 months · 15th"), next invoice, amount, status and when it runs, with the note on how runs work. "Recurring invoices" on the Invoices page opens it.
- **"+ New recurring invoice"** (`RecurringInvoiceDialog`): Name, Customer, Repeats (Weekly, Monthly, Every 3 months, Yearly), On (a weekday, the 1st–28th or the last day), Starts, Ends (Never, On a date, After a number of invoices), Payment due, "When it runs" (issue and email, or create a draft for review) and the lines (the invoice line editor, with a one-line summary). A start date in the past is refused: "Pick today or a later start date." A saved schedule is listed "Active" with its first invoice date.
- **Data.** `recurring_invoices` and `recurring_invoice_lines` (migration `0067`). The schedule maths (`occurrenceOnOrAfter`, `occurrenceAfter`, `scheduleFinished`) lives in `@spine/shared`. Nothing creates invoices yet; that's US-INV-21.
- **Permission.** New Team-configurable capability `invoices.manage_recurring` ("Manage recurring invoices", granted to General Staff by default; backfilled from `invoices.edit_own`). Without it the link is hidden and every `/recurring-invoices` route returns 403.

- `apps/e2e/tests/us-inv-20.spec.ts` (2): the dialog's fields, past start error, saved row; link hidden and 403 without the grant.

### 2026-10-05 — US-INV-19: send reminders on schedule without double-sending (Phase 7)

- **Daily run.** The `invoice-reminder-run` queue fires every 15 minutes. For each organisation with automatic reminders on, once organisation time (the server's `TZ`) passes "Send at", it runs the day once: every issued invoice with a balance whose due date is a scheduled number of days away gets one reminder email ("Reminder: invoice INV-… is 7 days overdue" / "is due today" / "is due in 3 days"). If the server was off at send time, the day runs once when it comes back. Missed days aren't caught up.
- **No double-sending.** New `invoice_reminders` table, one row per invoice per day (unique `invoice_id`, `sent_on`). Manual "Send reminder" records a row too, so the run skips an invoice already reminded that day. Paid, voided and fully credited invoices are never picked.
- **Pause per customer.** "Pause automatic reminders" switch on a customer's contact page (`contacts.reminders_paused`, through `PUT /contacts/:id` with `contacts.edit_*`). Vendors don't get the switch. Members who can't edit the contact see "Automatic reminders paused" instead.
- **History.** Each automatic reminder shows "Automatic reminder sent" in the invoice history.

Tests:
- `packages/shared/src/us-inv-19.test.ts` (2): the history line and the contact field.
- `apps/api/src/common/us-inv-19.test.ts` (2): organisation clock and day counting.
- `apps/api/src/modules/invoices/us-inv-19.http.integration.test.ts` (8): scheduled days, repeats, same-day skip (auto and manual), paid/void/credited skip, paused contact, once per day after "Send at", history, email subject/balance/instructions.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-inv-19.spec.ts` (2): the pause switch saves; vendors don't get it.

- **Settings › Automatic reminders** (Owner-only, the `SettingsReminders` design): "Send reminders automatically" (off), the schedule (3 days before, on the due date, 7 and 14 days after, and every 14 days after that up to 3 more times), "Send at" in half-hour steps (organisation time = the server's `TZ`), "Save" and "Preview email" (the email in a sandboxed frame with its subject).
- **Setting.** `invoice_reminders` `{ enabled, offsets, repeatEvery, repeatMax, sendAt }`, checked by `invoiceRemindersSchema` ("Pick at least one reminder."); D-091's defaults (due date, +7, +14 at 9:00) when unset; `reminderDays` works out every day a reminder is due.

(manual reminders too). No "View invoice" button: customers have no invoice page, so the PDF is attached (D-121 overrides the story here).

### 2026-10-05 — US-QUO-26: add a bundle while building a quote (Phase 7)

Choosing one replaces the line it was picked on with the bundle's lines, in order, with the bundle's prices and tax; each is an ordinary editable line. A line whose catalogue item has been archived is added without the item link (a one-off line). Only bundles in the document's currency are offered (`GET /catalog-bundles?currency=`).

Catalogue gets Items and Bundles tabs (`?tab=bundles`); Bundles lists name, description, lines, total and status with Edit, Archive/Restore and Delete.

Arrows, Enter and Escape work; focus leaving closes the list.

Saving the quote first creates an item for each marked line with a price (name, description, price and tax from the line; unit "each") through the existing `POST /catalog-items`, then saves the quote linked to it. ### 2026-10-05 — US-CRM-20: delete a stage and move its leads (Phase 7)

- **Settings › Pipeline stages** gains a red ✕ on every open stage. A stage with no leads is removed at once; one with leads opens "Delete “<stage>”?" with "Move <n> leads to:" and a stage picker, and "Delete stage" stays disabled until a stage is chosen. Moves wait until Save and are sent with the stages (`moves`), so nothing changes if you leave without saving; a stage that was itself a move target passes its incoming leads on.
- **API** (built with US-CRM-19): a deleted stage that still has leads is refused with '"<stage>" still has <n> leads. Choose a stage to move them to first.'; a move into a stage being deleted is refused; leads move and the stage is deleted in one transaction. Staff get 403.

### 2026-10-05 — US-CRM-19: rename, reorder and add pipeline stages (Phase 7)

- **Data.** New `lead_stages` table (name, kind open/won/lost, position, token colour) replaces the fixed `lead_stage` enum. Migration `0063` seeds New / Qualified / Proposal / Won / Lost for every organisation and maps each lead to the stage at its old position; `0064` drops the enum column. New organisations get the same stages at sign-up, and `LeadStagesService.ensureSeeded` covers any organisation without them.
- **API.** `GET/PUT /org/lead-stages` (read: any member; replace: Owner, idempotent). Rules live in `putLeadStagesSchema` (`@spine/shared`) so the form and API refuse the same things: up to 12, exactly one Won and one Lost, always last, distinct names; Won/Lost keep their kind and can't be removed. Each stage change is audited. Leads now carry `stageId`, `stageName`, `stageKind`; `summary-by-stage` returns one row per stage with its kind. The service already accepts `moves` for deleting a stage with leads; the delete control arrives with US-CRM-20.
- **Web.** Settings › Pipeline stages, laid out as the `LeadStagesSettings` design: drag handle (plus keyboard Move up/down buttons), colour dot, name field, kind, lead count, padlock on Won/Lost, "+ Add stage" (disabled at 12). The board's columns, filter and "Add lead", both lead forms, the contact Leads list, the Contacts open-lead count and the dashboard pipeline chart all read the organisation's stages.

Tests:
- `packages/shared/src/us-crm-19.test.ts` (5): defaults, accepted set, 12 limit, Won/Lost last and once, names.
- `apps/api/src/modules/contacts/us-crm-19.http.integration.test.ts` (8): seeded stages with counts; default stage on create; rename keeps leads; new stage at its position; 12 limit; Won/Lost rename only; another organisation's stage refused; summary by stage.

In dark mode a primary label is near-black on `#6B8AFF` (5.9:1) instead of white (3.1:1). Light mode looks the same as before.
- **Native.** `TextField`'s placeholder takes the mode's `onSurfaceMuted` from `useNativeColors()` (`#93907F` in dark, was a fixed `#726C5A`). Avatar initials and the iOS full-width button label use `onPrimary` / `onDanger`. The gap noted in `native-notes.md` is closed.
- **Tests.** A contrast test fails if any on-*/fill pair is under 4.5:1 in either mode, in the tokens, the web CSS or the native map. Every `@spine/ui` axe test now runs in both `data-theme="light"` and `"dark"`.

Tests:
- `packages/ui/src/components/us-ops-25.contrast.test.ts` (7): on-primary/primary and on-danger/overdue at ≥ 4.5:1 in both modes in all three sources; light stays white; the old white-on-dark-primary was below AA; the native dark placeholder is readable.
- All 79 `@spine/ui` a11y tests now run in both themes.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-ops-25.spec.ts` (2): the "Log in" button's computed text and fill colours in dark and light mode.

### 2026-10-05 — US-OPS-24: show money the same way everywhere (Phase 6)

- **One formatter.** `formatMoney(minor, currency, locale)` in `@spine/shared`, built on `Intl.NumberFormat`. It gives the currency symbol, the locale's grouping and decimal marks, the currency's own decimals and a true minus sign: "$12,024.75", "−$4,335.00", "₹1,20,247.50", "12.024,75 €". Amounts stay exact (decimal strings, never floats).
- **Web.** About 100 call sites in 55 files now use it: lists, details, builders, dashboard widgets, reports, receivables and payables, the public quote page. They used to show "12024.75 USD" or plain numbers.
  - **Locale.** Comes from Settings › Currency & locale. The server layout reads it once and `MoneyLocaleProvider` sets it before anything renders. The public quote API returns the business's `locale`.
  - **Columns.** Money columns are right-aligned, with tabular figures in the normal UI font (no more monospace), in the web app and in `@spine/ui`'s chart, donut and money input.
  - **Negatives.** Credit notes, discounts, retention, paid and credited amounts show "−$…" instead of a hand-typed "-".
- **PDFs** (quote, invoice, receipt, credit note) use the same formatter. They have an en dash for the minus, and the currency code when Helvetica has no symbol for it (D-123).
- **Emails and notifications.** Email facts, payment and expense notifications, the activity feed and the out-of-balance warning use the organisation's locale. CSV exports keep plain decimals on purpose (US-PAY-10).
- **Lint rule.** `@spine/config/eslint/money`, applied to `apps/web` and the API's PDF and email code, fails on importing `toDecimalString` and on joining an amount with a currency code (`${amount} ${currency}` or `{amount} {currency}`). It caught the catalogue price showing its currency twice.
- **Found along the way (D-124).** Rate limits counted per IP address, and the web app's server-side calls (session, membership, locale) all come from the web server's one address. So the whole team shared one bucket of 300 requests a minute, and the full E2E run tripped it ("ThrottlerException: Too Many Requests"). `SessionThrottlerGuard` now counts signed-in requests per session and public routes per IP.

Tests:
- `packages/shared/src/money/us-ops-24.test.ts` (5): the story's examples in en-US, en-IN and de-DE, the true minus, JPY decimals, exactness beyond 2^53, fallbacks.
- `us-ops-24.test.ts` (API, 3): PDF money (symbol, en dash, code fallback). `us-ops-24-lint.test.ts` (4): the lint rule fails on template strings, JSX and the import, and passes `formatMoney`.
- `session-throttler.http.integration.test.ts` (2): one user's limit doesn't throttle another on the same address; random cookies don't get around a public route's limit.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-ops-24.spec.ts` (1): invoice amounts read "$1,234.50" with no " USD", and switch to "1.234,50 $" after changing the locale to de-DE.
- Updated for the new format: email snapshots, `us-ntf-10`, `us-rpt-12`, `quote-to-payment` and `us-ntf-10` E2E.

Entries read like "Sarah Mendes changed the phone from +1-555-0107 to +1-555-0177", "set the email to …" or "cleared the tax id". Field names are written for people ("total amount"), money fields are decimals, and internal ids are left out.

It has a "How customers can pay" field ("Up to 600 characters. Leave empty to hide the block."), the note "Issued invoices keep the instructions they were issued with — changing this only affects invoices issued from now on." and "Save".

### 2026-10-04 — US-QUO-22: keep emailed links working until the quote is replaced (Phase 6)

- **Finding C8, explained.** The demo's fourth quote (QUO-0003) is seeded as "revised": it was sent and emailed, then revised. Revising revokes every link for the old version, and that's intended. But the revision was left as a draft, so the quote still read "sent" while its only emailed link was dead.
- **Fix.** The demo now sends the revision as well. The current version is "sent" with a working emailed link; the replaced version's link shows the branded "This quote link has expired" page (US-QUO-21).
- **Confirmed unchanged.** A link lasts 30 days. Issuing another link for the same quote never revokes the earlier ones; a queued email retry reuses its own token. Only revising a quote revokes its links. There is no separate "resend" action that could rotate tokens.

Tests:
- `us-quo-22.http.integration.test.ts` (4): every demo quote that's sent and current has an emailed link that opens; the revised demo quote's old link answers 410 "expired" while its sent revision's link opens; two links for one quote both work; a link lasts 30 days.

### 2026-10-04 — US-QUO-21: show a branded page for an expired or invalid link (Phase 6)

- **Expired or replaced.** A quote link we issued that has run out (30 days), been revoked or replaced by a revision now shows the business logo and name, "This quote link has expired" and "The link you opened is no longer active — the quote may have been updated or replaced. Contact <Business> and they'll send you the latest version.". A box follows with the business's address, email (mailto) and phone (tel), and "Sent with Spine".
- **Invalid.** A token that matches nothing (not ours, bad signature, unknown) shows a generic "This quote link isn't valid" with no business details.
- **API.** `GET /public/quotes/:token` now answers 410 `{ status: "expired", business }` or 404 `{ status: "invalid" }` instead of one generic 404. `QuotePublicTokenService.inspect()` tells the two apart: an expired token's payload is trusted only after its signature checks out and its nonce exists. The logo comes inline as a data URI, because anonymous visitors can't load `/files/:id`.
- **Business profile.** Gains "Business email" and "Business phone" (Settings › Business profile and onboarding). Saving the profile now keeps fields set elsewhere, such as payment instructions.

Tests:
- `us-quo-21.http.integration.test.ts` (5): a current link loads; a revoked link and a link past 30 days answer 410 with name, address, email and phone; tokens that match nothing, and a token signed with another key, answer 404 with no business details.
- `apps/e2e/tests/us-quo-21.spec.ts` (2): a revised quote's old link shows the branded page with mailto and tel links; a made-up token shows "This quote link isn't valid". `public-quote-page.spec.ts` updated for the new wording.

It has a 6px bar in the document colour, the logo and business name, a heading, a short body, an optional key-facts box, at most one button, small print, and the business footer (name · address · branding footer). Colours are the light token values, inlined; emails stay light.

With images blocked, the business name still shows as text and is the logo's alt text (D-120).

Reply to this email.". The From name is the business name; the address is still `SMTP_FROM`.
